In a trice the surly crowds had vanished. Instead of these, there were groups of gaily-visaged men pleasantly chattering outside every eating and drinking place in the town. The national holiday had come upon these people quite unawares, so the early part of it had to be spent in thinking out a satisfactory programme for it. Sipping their beer or coffee, or munching their cherries à l’eau-de-vie, the townsfolk of Boulogne, so lately threatened with death, were quietly organizing processions.

===Chinese===
不管他是什么——英雄也好，奸细也好——毋庸置疑，他们这一场欢喜，全然是拜他所赐。

等到奥古斯特·莫勒把这消息喊遍了全城，把那道新的恩赦公告钉上每一座公共建筑，疲乏与忧惧的痕迹早已荡然无存。虽说那一夜家家户户都守了难熬的夜，几百号男女在加约勒堡垒四周一站就是好几个钟头，可喜讯一经传开，一切困倦顿忘，人人兴冲冲地动手张罗，定要叫这个盛大的节日办得十全十美。

诺曼底人，无论庄稼汉还是体面先生，骨子里都有一种享乐的无穷本领，同时又有一手把享乐安排妥帖、调理井然的绝妙功夫。

转眼之间，那些阴郁的人群便无影无踪了。取而代之的，是一伙伙满面喜气的男人，在全城各家酒肆食铺门外惬意地闲谈。这个全国性假日来得全然出其不意，于是头半天便只好用来琢磨出一套称心的节日章程。呷着啤酒或咖啡，嚼着酒渍樱桃（cherries à l’eau-de-vie），这些不久前还受着死亡威胁的布洛涅城里人，不慌不忙地张罗起游行来。

===Original===
There was to be a grand muster on the Place de la Sénéchaussée, then a torchlight and lantern-light march, right round the Ramparts, culminating in a gigantic assembly outside the Town Hall, where the Citizen Chauvelin, representing the Committee of Public Safety, would receive an address of welcome from the entire population of Boulogne.

The procession was to be in costume! There were to be Pierrots and Pierettes, Harlequins and English clowns, aristocrats and goddesses! All day the women and girls were busy contriving travesties of all sorts, and the little tumbledown shops in the Rue de Château and the Rue Frédéric Sauvage—kept chiefly by Jews and English traders—were ransacked for old bits of finery, and for remnants of costumes, worn in the days when Boulogne was still a gay city and Carnivals were held every year.

And then, of course, there would be the Goddess of Reason, in her triumphal car! the apotheosis of the new religion, which was to make everybody happy, rich and free.

Forgotten were the anxieties of the night, the fears of death, the great and glorious Revolution, which for this one day would cease her perpetual demand for the toll of blood.

Nothing was remembered save the pleasures and joys of the moment, and at times the name of that Englishman—spy, hero or adventurer—the cause of all this bounty: the Scarlet Pimpernel.

===Chinese===
章程是先在塞内绍斯广场大集合，然后打着火把、提着灯笼游行，绕城墙整整一周，最后在市政厅外面举行盛大集会：肖夫兰公民将代表公共安全委员会，接受全布洛涅居民敬献的欢迎辞。

游行还要化装！要有皮埃罗（Pierrot）和皮埃蕾特（Pierrette），有阿尔莱坎（Harlequin）和英国小丑，有贵族和女神！一整天，妇女和姑娘们忙着缝制各式各样的奇装异服；城堡街（Rue de Château）和弗雷德里克·索瓦日街（Rue Frédéric Sauvage）那些东倒西歪的小铺子——多半是犹太人和英国商人开的——被人翻了个底朝天，搜寻旧日的零碎华丽衣饰、演戏行头的残片，那是布洛涅还算个快活城市、年年举办狂欢节的年月里穿过的。

再者，当然还得有理性女神，乘着她的凯旋车！这门新宗教的封神大典——这门宗教保准叫人人幸福、人人富有、人人自由。

忘却了，昨夜的忧惧，对死亡的恐怖，还有那伟大而光荣的革命——仅仅这一天，她不再永无止境地索要血的献祭。

人心里只记得眼下的赏心乐事，偶尔还惦记起那个英国人的名字——奸细也罢，英雄也罢，冒险家也罢——这一切恩泽的总因头：猩红石榴红。

## XXX. The Procession / XXX. 巡游

===Original===
The grandfathers of the present generation of Boulonnese remembered the great day of the National Fête, when all Boulogne, for twenty-four hours, went crazy with joy. So many families had fathers, brothers, sons, languishing in prison under some charge of treason, real or imaginary; so many had dear ones for whom already the guillotine loomed ahead, that the feast on this memorable day of September, 1793, was one of never-to-be-forgotten relief and thanksgiving.

===Chinese===
如今这一代布洛涅人的祖辈，都还记得全国庆典那个盛大的日子——那一天，整个布洛涅欢欢喜喜闹了二十四个小时，快乐得发了狂。多少人家，父兄子侄顶着或真或假的叛国罪名在牢里熬煎；多少人家，至亲至爱的人眼前已隐隐逼来断头台的影子。于是，1793 年 9 月这个难忘日子的庆典，便成了一场永生难忘的宽慰与感恩。

===Original===
The weather all day had been exceptionally fine. After that glorious sunrise, the sky had remained all day clad in its gorgeous mantle of blue and the sun had continued to smile benignly on the many varied doings of this gay, little seaport town. When it began to sink slowly towards the West a few little fluffy clouds appeared on the horizon, and from a distance, although the sky remained clear and blue, the sea looked quite dark and slaty against the brilliance of the firmament.

Gradually, as the splendour of the sunset gave place to the delicate purple and grey tints of evening, the little fluffy clouds merged themselves into denser masses, and these too soon became absorbed in the great, billowy banks which the southwesterly wind was blowing seawards.

By the time that the last grey streak of dusk vanished in the West, the whole sky looked heavy with clouds, and the evening set in, threatening and dark.

===Chinese===
这一天，天气出奇地晴好。壮丽的日出之后，天空终日披着华美的蓝氅，太阳一直和颜悦色，笑望着这座快活小海港城里五花八门的行止。待到日头缓缓沉向西方，天边浮起几朵绒毛似的小云；远远望去，天虽依旧澄澈湛蓝，大海映着穹苍的明辉，却显得深暗发灰。

日落的辉煌渐渐让位给暮色淡雅的紫灰，绒毛般的小云团凝成愈发厚密的云堆，不多时，这些云堆也一并没入西南风卷向大海的滚滚云阵。

待黄昏最后一抹灰光在西天消隐，满天云层已沉沉欲坠；夜色四合，阴黑而带着威胁。

===Original===
But this by no means mitigated the anticipation of pleasure to come. On the contrary, the fast-gathering gloom was hailed with delight, since it would surely help to show off the coloured lights of the lanterns, and give additional value to the glow of the torches.

Of a truth 'twas a motley throng which began to assemble on the Place de la Sénéchaussée, just as the old bell of the Beffroi tolled the hour of six. Men, women and children in ragged finery, Pierrots with neck frills and floured faces, hideous masks of impossible beasts roughly besmeared in crude colours. There were gaily-coloured dominoes, blue, green, pink and purple, harlequins combining all the colours of the rainbow in one tight-fitting garment, and Columbines with short, tarlatan skirts, beneath which peeped bare feet and ankles. There were judges' perruques, and soldiers' helmets of past generations, tall Normandy caps adorned with hundreds of streaming ribbons, and powdered headgear which recalled the glories of Versailles.

Everything was torn and dirty, the dominoes were in rags, the Pierrot frills, mostly made up of paper, already hung in strips over the wearers' shoulders. But what mattered that?

The crowd pushed and jolted, shouted and laughed, the girls screamed as the men snatched a kiss here and there from willing or unwilling lips, or stole an arm round a gaily accoutred waist. The spirit of Old King Carnival was in the evening air — a spirit just awakened from a long Rip van Winkle-like sleep.

===Chinese===
但这丝毫没有冲淡人们对将临之乐的期盼。恰恰相反，急速聚拢的阴霾反倒招人欢喜：它准能把彩灯的颜色衬得愈发鲜亮，让火把的光焰更添异彩。

千真万确，老贝夫鲁瓦钟楼的古钟刚敲响六点，塞内绍斯广场上便聚起了五色斑斓的一大群人。男男女女、大人孩子，个个破衣烂衫里裹着一身盛饰：皮埃罗（Pierrot）们戴着颈褶、扑着白粉脸；还有种种子虚乌有的怪兽假面，狰狞可怖，涂满粗劣的彩色。有五颜六色的化装斗篷，蓝的、绿的、粉的、紫的；有哈利昆（Harlequin）们，一身紧身衣裳集齐了彩虹的全部颜色；有科隆比娜（Columbine）们，短短的塔勒坦纱裙底下，露出光脚和脚踝。有法官的假发，有前朝兵卒的头盔，有缀着千百条飘带的诺曼底高帽，还有扑满发粉、教人想起凡尔赛旧日荣光的种种头饰。

件件衣裳都又破又脏：化装斗篷成了碎条，皮埃罗的颈褶多半是纸糊的，早已一条条挂在肩头。可这又有什么要紧？

人群推推搡搡，又叫又笑；男人们冷不防从那些或情愿或不情愿的唇上抢个香吻，或是悄悄伸胳膊搂一把盛装打扮的腰肢，惹得姑娘们尖声叫喊。晚风里荡漾着狂欢老王（Old King Carnival）的魂灵——一个刚从瑞普·凡·温克尔式长眠中醒来的魂灵。

===Original===
In the centre of the Place stood the guillotine, grim and gaunt with long, thin arms stretched out towards the sky, the last glimmer of waning light striking the triangular knife, there, where it was not rusty with stains of blood.

For weeks now Madame Guillotine had been much occupied plying her gruesome trade; she now stood there in the gloom, passive and immovable, seeming to wait placidly for the end of this holiday, ready to begin her work again on the morrow. She towered above these merrymakers, hoisted up on the platform whereon many an innocent foot had trodden, the tattered basket beside her, into which many an innocent head had rolled.

What cared they tonight for Madame Guillotine and the horrors of which she told? A crowd of Pierrots with floured faces and tattered neck-frills had just swarmed up the wooden steps, shouting and laughing, chasing each other round and round on the platform, until one of them lost his footing and fell into the basket, covering himself with bran and staining his clothes with blood.

===Chinese===
广场中央立着断头台，狰狞而瘦削，细长的臂架伸向天空；残照最后一抹微光正落在那三角铡刀上——落在刀身上尚未被血锈漫过的地方。

几个星期以来，断头台夫人（Madame Guillotine）一直忙着做她那门阴森的营生；此刻她立在昏暗里，沉静而纹丝不动，仿佛安然等着这个节日过完，只待明天重新开工。她高高耸立在寻欢作乐的人群之上，架在那许多无辜之脚踏过的平台上，身旁放着那只破篮子——多少无辜的头颅曾滚落其中。

今夜谁还理会断头台夫人，理会她诉说的种种恐怖？一群白粉脸、破颈褶的皮埃罗，刚蜂拥着爬上木阶，又叫又笑地在平台上绕圈追逐，直到其中一个脚下一滑，跌进篮子里，扑了一身麸皮，衣裳也染上了血污。

===Original===
"Ah! vogue la galère! We must be merry tonight!"

And all these people who for weeks past had been staring death and the guillotine in the face, had denounced each other with savage callousness in order to save themselves, or hidden for days in dark cellars to escape apprehension, now laughed, and danced and shrieked with gladness in a sudden, hysterical outburst of joy.

===Chinese===
「啊！由它去吧，管它明日天翻地覆！（*vogue la galère*）今晚咱们非乐个痛快不可！」

这些人，几个星期来一直同死亡和断头台面对面瞪视，为保全自己曾以野蛮的冷酷互相告发，或为躲避缉捕在地窖里一连藏上数日，此刻却在骤然发作的癔症般的狂喜里，又是笑，又是跳，又是乐得尖声怪叫。

===Original===
Close beside the guillotine stood the triumphal car of the Goddess of Reason, the special feature of this great national fête. It was only a rough market cart, painted by an unpractised hand with bright, crimson paint and adorned with huge clusters of autumn-tinted leaves, and the scarlet berries of mountain ash and rowan, culled from the town gardens, or the country side outside the city walls.

In the cart the goddess reclined on a crimson-draped seat, she, herself, swathed in white, and wearing a gorgeous necklace around her neck. Désirée Candeille, a little pale, a little apprehensive of all this noise, had obeyed the final dictates of her taskmaster. She had been the means of bringing the Scarlet Pimpernel to France and vengeance, she was to be honoured therefore above every other woman in France.

She sat in the car, vaguely thinking over the events of the past few days, whilst watching the throng of rowdy merrymakers seething around her. She thought of the noble-hearted, proud woman whom she had helped to bring from her beautiful English home to sorrow and humiliation in a dank French prison, she thought of the gallant English gentleman with his pleasant voice and courtly, debonnair manners.

===Chinese===
紧挨着断头台，立着理性女神的凯旋彩车，这是这场盛大全国庆典上特设的重头戏。那不过是一辆粗陋的集市板车，让生手漆上了鲜亮的猩红漆，缀着大团大团染上秋色的枝叶，还有花楸和山梨那猩红的果子——都是从城中园圃或城墙外的乡野间采来的。

车上，女神斜倚在猩红铺裹的座位上，她本人裹着一身白，颈间挂着一串华丽的项链。德西蕾·康代伊脸色微微发白，对这一片喧嚣微微有些心怯，终究还是遵从了主子最后的吩咐。是她设法把猩红石榴红引进了法兰西、送到了复仇之口，因此她将受尊崇，凌驾于法兰西一切女子之上。

她坐在车里，望着周围沸反盈天的一群喧闹狂欢者，恍恍惚惚回想这几日的种种。她想到那位心地高贵、性情骄傲的夫人——自己曾帮着把她从美丽的英国家园拖进法兰西湿冷的牢狱、拖进悲哀与羞辱；她想到那位英武的英国绅士，那悦耳的嗓音，那彬彬有礼、风流倜傥的做派。

===Original===
Chauvelin had roughly told her, only this morning, that both were now under arrest as English spies, and that their fate no longer concerned her. Later on the governor of the city had come to tell her that Citizen Chauvelin desired her to take part in the procession and the national fête, as the Goddess of Reason, and that the people of Boulogne were ready to welcome her as such. This had pleased Candeille's vanity, and all day, whilst arranging the finery which she meant to wear for the occasion, she had ceased to think of England and of Lady Blakeney.

But now, when she arrived on the Place de la Sénéchaussée, and mounting her car, found herself on a level with the platform of the guillotine, her memory flew back to England, to the lavish hospitality of Blakeney Manor, Marguerite's gentle voice, the pleasing grace of Sir Percy's manners, and she shuddered a little when that cruel glint of evening light caused the knife of the guillotine to glisten from out the gloom.

===Chinese===
就在今天早上，肖夫兰还粗声粗气地对她说：那两人如今已作为英国间谍被捕，他们的命运与她再无干系。随后，本城总督又来传话，说肖夫兰公民有意让她以理性女神之身参加巡游和全国庆典，布洛涅的百姓已准备好如此迎奉她。这话正搔着了康代伊的虚荣心；一整天，她张罗着预备穿去赴会的漂亮行头，早把英格兰、把布莱克尼夫人丢在了脑后。

可此刻，当她来到塞内绍斯广场，登上彩车，发觉自己恰与断头台的平台齐平，记忆便倏地飞回了英格兰：布莱克尼庄园里丰盛慷慨的殷勤款待，玛格丽特温柔的声音，珀西爵士风度中那悦人的优雅。暮色里一道无情的光闪过，断头台的铡刀从幽暗中亮出脸来，她不由得微微打了个寒噤。

===Original===
But anon her reflections were suddenly interrupted by loud and prolonged shouts of joy. A whole throng of Pierrots had swarmed into the Place from every side, carrying lighted torches and tall staves, on which were hung lanterns with many-coloured lights.

The procession was ready to start. A stentorian voice shouted out in resonant accents:

"*En avant, la grosse caisse!*"

===Chinese===
可不一会儿，阵阵高亢持久的欢呼骤然打断了她的思绪。一大群皮埃罗从四面八方涌进广场，举着点亮的火把和高高的长杆，杆上挂着流光溢彩的灯笼。

巡游就要出发了。一个洪钟般的嗓门朗声高喊：

「前进，大鼓开路！」（*En avant, la grosse caisse!*）

===Original===
A man now, portly and gorgeous in scarlet and blue, detached himself from out the crowd. His head was hidden beneath the monstrous mask of a cardboard lion, roughly painted in brown and yellow, with crimson for the widely open jaws and the corners of the eyes, to make them seem ferocious and bloodshot. His coat was of bright crimson cloth, with cuts and slashings in it, through which bunches of bright blue paper were made to protrude, in imitation of the costume of medieval times.

He had blue stockings on and bright scarlet slippers, and behind him floated a large strip of scarlet flannel, on which moons and suns and stars of gold had been showered in plenty.

Upon his portly figure in front he was supporting the big drum, which was securely strapped round his shoulders with tarred cordages, the spoil of some fishing vessel.

===Chinese===
这时，一个身躯肥硕的男人从人群里闪身而出，一身红蓝，气派十足。他的脑袋罩在一只纸板狮子的大假面底下，假面用棕、黄两色草草涂成，大张的狮口和眼角涂着猩红，好显得凶恶而布满血丝。他的外套是鲜红的呢料，上头开着道道切口裂缝，口子里塞出一簇簇鲜蓝的纸片，模仿中世纪的衣装。

他脚上穿蓝长袜、鲜红便鞋，身后飘着一大条猩红法兰绒，上面泼洒了数不清的金月亮、金太阳、金星星。

那肥硕的身躯前头兜着大鼓，鼓用浸过焦油的缆绳牢牢捆在肩上——缆绳是从哪条渔船上缴获来的。

===Original===
There was a merciful slit in the jaw of the cardboard lion, through which the portly drummer puffed and spluttered as he shouted lustily:

"En avant!"

And wielding the heavy drumstick with a powerful arm, he brought it crashing down against the side of the mighty instrument.

===Chinese===
纸板狮子的下颌上开着一道慈悲的缝，肥硕的鼓手便从那缝里一面喘气喷唾沫，一面扯开嗓门高喊：

「前进！」

说罢，他抡起强壮的臂膀挥动沉重的鼓槌，砰的一声砸在大鼓的鼓帮上。

===Original===
"Hurrah! Hurrah! *en avant les trompettes!*"

A fanfare of brass instruments followed, lustily blown by twelve young men in motley coats of green, and tall, peaked hats adorned with feathers.

===Chinese===
「好哇！好哇！喇叭手们，上！」（*en avant les trompettes!*）

紧接着响起一阵铜管号角，十二个年轻小伙起劲地吹着，个个身穿杂色的绿上衣，头戴饰有羽毛的高尖帽。

===Original===
The drummer had begun to march, closely followed by the trumpeters. Behind them a bevy of Columbines in many-coloured tarlatan skirts and hair flying wildly in the breeze, giggling, pushing, exchanging ribald jokes with the men behind, and getting kissed or slapped for their pains.

Then the triumphal car of the goddess, with Demoiselle Candeille standing straight up in it, a tall gold wand in one hand, the other resting in a mass of scarlet berries. All round the car, helter-skelter, tumbling, pushing, came Pierrots and Pierrettes, carrying lanterns, and Harlequins bearing the torches.

===Chinese===
鼓手开步前行，吹号手紧随其后。再后面是一群科隆比娜，穿着五颜六色的塔勒坦纱裙，头发在风里狂飞乱舞，咯咯笑着、推搡着，同后头的男人们插科打诨说荤话，凑趣的报偿，不是挨个香吻，就是挨记巴掌。

再后面是女神的凯旋彩车，康代伊小姐端端正正立在车上，一手握着高高的金杖，另一只手插在满捧的猩红浆果里。彩车四周，皮埃罗和皮埃罗特（Pierrette）们提着灯笼，哈利昆们擎着火把，乱纷纷地连滚带爬、推推挤挤涌在一处。

===Original===
And after the car the long line of more sober folk, the older fishermen, the women in caps and many-hued skirts, the serious townfolk who had scorned the travesty, yet would not be left out of the procession. They all began to march, to the tune of those noisy brass trumpets which were thundering forth snatches from the newly composed "Marseillaise."

Above the sky became more heavy with clouds. Anon a few drops of rain began to fall, making the torches sizzle and splutter, and scatter grease and tar around and wetting the lightly-covered shoulders of tarlatan-clad Columbines. But no one cared! The glow of so much merrymaking kept the blood warm and the skin dry.

===Chinese===
车后是长长一列较为持重的人：上了年纪的老渔夫，戴便帽、穿彩裙的妇人，还有那些正经的城里人——他们本瞧不上这等滑稽把戏，却又不甘被巡游落下。众人齐齐开步，合着喧闹铜号的调子——那号角正轰鸣着吹出新近谱成的《马赛曲》（Marseillaise）的片段。

头顶的天空，云层愈发沉重。不多时，雨点开始落下，浇得火把嗤嗤作响、噼啪乱溅，油脂和焦油洒了一路，也打湿了塔勒坦纱裙的科隆比娜们轻掩的香肩。可谁在乎！这般欢腾的火光，烘得人血热肤干。

===Original===
The flour all came off the Pierrots' faces, the blue paper slashings of the drummer-in-chief hung in pulpy lumps against his gorgeous scarlet cloak. The trumpeters' feathers became streaky and bedraggled.

But in the name of that good God who had ceased to exist, who in the world or out of it cared if it rained, or thundered and stormed! This was a national holiday, for an English spy was captured, and all natives of Boulogne were free of the guillotine tonight.

The revellers were making the circuit of the town, with lanterns fluttering in the wind, and flickering torches held up aloft illumining laughing faces, red with the glow of a drunken joy, young faces that only enjoyed the moment's pleasure, serious ones that withheld a frown at thought of the morrow. The fitful light played on the grotesque masques of beasts and reptiles, on the diamond necklace of a very earthly goddess, on God's glorious spoils from gardens and countryside, on smothered anxiety and repressed cruelty.

===Chinese===
皮埃罗脸上的白粉掉了个精光，总鼓手身上的蓝纸条浸成了烂糊，一坨坨粘在他华丽的猩红披风上。号手帽上的羽毛也湿成一绺绺，狼狈地耷拉着。

可是，凭那位已经不复存在的好上帝起誓——世上也罢、世外也罢，谁管它下雨不下雨、打雷不打雷、狂风不狂风！这是举国同庆的假日：逮住了一个英国间谍，今夜凡布洛涅本地人，一律免了断头之灾。

寻欢的人们绕城巡游，灯笼在风里翻飞，忽闪的火把高高举起，照亮一张张笑脸：有的笑得通红，透着醉醺醺的狂喜；有的年轻，只图眼前片刻的欢愉；也有严肃的脸，一想到明天，便强忍着不蹙起眉头。忽明忽暗的光，戏弄着走兽爬虫的怪诞假面，戏弄着这位十足人间女神的钻石项链，戏弄着上帝从园圃田野掠来的荣美战利品，也戏弄着压抑的焦灼与收敛的狠戾。

===Original===
The crowd had turned its back on the guillotine, and the trumpets now changed the inspiriting tune of the "Marseillaise" to the ribald vulgarity of the "*Ça ira.*"

Everyone yelled and shouted. Girls with flowing hair produced broomsticks, and astride on these, broke from the ranks and danced a mad and obscene saraband, a dance of witches in the weird glow of sizzling torches, to the accompaniment of raucous laughter and of coarse jokes.

Thus the procession passed on, a sight to gladden the eyes of those who had desired to smother all thought of the Infinite, of Eternity and of God in the minds of those to whom they had nothing to offer in return. A threat of death yesterday, misery, starvation and squalor! all the hideousness of a destroying anarchy, that had nothing to give save a national fête, a tinsel goddess, some shallow laughter and momentary intoxication, a travesty of clothes and of religion and a dance on the ashes of the past.

===Chinese===
人群早已把断头台抛在背后；铜号这时也换了曲子，从振奋人心的《马赛曲》，换成了粗鄙淫俗的《Ça ira》。

人人又叫又嚷。长发披散的姑娘们抽出扫帚把，跨骑上去，冲出队伍，跳起疯狂而淫邪的萨拉班德舞（saraband）——那是女巫之舞，在嗤嗤作响的火把诡异的光里，伴着刺耳的哄笑和粗俗的戏语。

巡游队伍就这样一路行进——这景象，正叫某些人看了称心快意：他们对自己拿不出半点报偿的百姓，就巴望闷死他们心里一切关于无限、关于永恒、关于上帝的念头。昨日是死亡的威胁，是苦难、饥饿与污秽！这就是毁灭性无政府状态的全部丑恶——它拿不出别的，只有一场全国庆典、一个锡箔女神、几声浅薄的欢笑和片刻的沉醉，一场衣裳与宗教的滑稽戏，还有踩在过去灰烬上的一支舞。

===Original===
And there along the ramparts where the massive walls of the city encircled the frowning prisons of Gayole and the old Château, dark groups were crouching, huddled together in compact masses, which in the gloom seemed to vibrate with fear. Like hunted quarry seeking for shelter, sombre figures flattened themselves in the angles of the dank walls, as the noisy carousers drew nigh. Then as the torches and lanterns detached themselves from out the evening shadows, hand would clutch hand and hearts would beat with agonized suspense, whilst the dark and shapeless forms would try to appear smaller, flatter, less noticeable than before.

And when the crowd had passed noisily along, leaving behind it a trail of torn finery, of glittering tinsel and of scarlet berries, when the boom of the big drum and the grating noise of the brass trumpets had somewhat died away, wan faces, pale with anxiety, would peer from out the darkness, and nervous hands would grasp with trembling fingers the small bundles of poor belongings tied up hastily in view of flight.

===Chinese===
而在城墙根一带——高大的城墙圈着加约勒和老城堡这两座阴森的监狱——暗处蜷伏着一团团黑影，挤作紧实的一堆堆，在幽暗里仿佛因恐惧而瑟瑟颤动。喧闹的纵乐人群一逼近，便有人像被追猎的困兽觅着藏身之处，把阴沉沉的身影贴进湿墙的夹角。待火把和灯笼从夜影里脱颖而出，手便攥紧了手，心便揪得突突乱跳，那些黑黢黢、辨不出形状的人影，还竭力想显得更小些、更扁些，比先前更不惹眼。

待人群喧喧嚷嚷地过去，留下一路撕破的华服、闪闪的锡箔和散落的猩红浆果；待大鼓的轰鸣和铜号刺耳的声响渐渐远去，一张张焦灼的苍白面孔才从黑暗里探出来，一双双紧张的手用发抖的指头攥紧小包袱——那是为了随时出逃而匆匆打点的寒酸家当。

===Original===
At seven o'clock, so 'twas said, the cannon would boom from the old Beffroi. The guard would throw open the prison gates, and those who had something or somebody to hide, and those who had a great deal to fear, would be free to go whithersoever they chose.

And mothers, sisters, sweethearts stood watching by the gates, for loved ones tonight would be set free, all along of the capture of that English spy, the Scarlet Pimpernel.

===Chinese===
据说，到了七点，老贝夫鲁瓦钟楼便会响起炮声。守军将敞开监狱大门，那些有东西要藏、有人要藏的，那些心虚害怕得厉害的，从此便自由了，爱往哪儿去就往哪儿去。

母亲们、姐妹们、心上人们，都伫立在狱门旁守望：今夜亲人就要获释——全亏逮住了那个英国间谍，猩红石榴红。

## XXXI. Final Dispositions / XXXI. 最后部署

===Original===
To Chauvelin the day had been one of restless inquietude and nervous apprehension.

===Chinese===
这一天，对肖夫兰而言，是一整天的坐立不安与提心吊胆。

===Original===
Collot d’Herbois harassed him with questions and complaints intermixed with threats but thinly veiled. At his suggestion Gayole had been transformed into a fully-manned, well-garrisoned fortress. Troops were to be seen everywhere, on the stairs and in the passages, the guardrooms and offices: picked men from the municipal guard, and the company which had been sent down from Paris some time ago.

===Chinese===
科洛·德埃尔布瓦拿问话和抱怨轮番纠缠他，其间夹着仅只薄薄一层遮掩的威胁。在他的主张下，加约勒已被改造成一座兵员齐备、守备森严的堡垒。楼梯上、过道里、卫兵室和办公房中，处处都见得到兵：既有从城防军里拣选出来的精壮，也有前些时从巴黎调来的那支连队。

===Original===
Chauvelin had not resisted these orders given by his colleague. He knew quite well that Marguerite would make no attempt at escape, but he had long ago given up all hope of persuading a man of the type of Collot d’Herbois that a woman of her temperament would never think of saving her own life at the expense of others, and that Sir Percy Blakeney, in spite of his adoration for his wife, would sooner see her die before him, than allow the lives of innocent men and women to be the price of hers.

===Chinese===
同僚下的这些命令，肖夫兰未作抗拒。他心里很明白，玛格丽特绝不会图谋越狱；可要叫科洛·德埃尔布瓦这一号人相信——相信她那样性情的女子，断不会想着拿旁人性命换自己活命；相信珀西·布莱克尼爵士纵然爱妻入骨，也宁可眼睁睁看她死在自己面前，绝不容无辜男女的性命去做她一命的赎价——这样的说服，他老早就死了心了。

===Original===
Collot was one of those brutish sots﻿—not by any means infrequent among the Terrorists of that time﻿—who, born in the gutter, still loved to wallow in his native element, and who measured all his fellow-creatures by the same standard which he had always found good enough for himself. In this man there was neither the enthusiastic patriotism of a Chauvelin, nor the ardent selflessness of a Danton. He served the revolution and fostered the anarchical spirit of the times only because these brought him a competence and a notoriety, which an orderly and fastidious government would obviously have never offered him.

===Chinese===
科洛是那种粗鄙的酒囊饭袋——在当时的恐怖党人里绝非罕物——生在阴沟，仍旧爱在娘胎里的泥淖中打滚，又拿他自己一向受用惯了的那杆尺，去量度天下同类。这个人身上，既没有肖夫兰那种狂热的爱国心，也没有丹东那种炽烈的无私。他效命革命、鼓煽时下的无政府风气，只因这两样替他挣来了进项和恶名——换一个井然有序、讲究体面的政府，这些显然是断然轮不到他的。

===Original===
History shows no more despicable personality than that of Collot d’Herbois, one of the most hideous products of that utopian Revolution, whose grandly conceived theories of a universal levelling of mankind only succeeded in dragging into prominence a number of half-brutish creatures who, revelling in their own abasement, would otherwise have remained content in inglorious obscurity.

===Chinese===
历史上再寻不出比科洛·德埃尔布瓦更可鄙的人物了。他是那场乌托邦革命产下的最丑恶的怪胎之一——那场革命堂而皇之的全民齐平学说，到头来只是把一批半人半兽的东西拽到了显赫之地；这路货色沉醉于自身的下贱，若非如此，原会心满意足地烂在无闻的暗处。

===Original===
Chauvelin tolerated and half feared Collot, knowing full well that if now the Scarlet Pimpernel escaped from his hands, he could expect no mercy from his colleagues.

===Chinese===
肖夫兰容得下科洛，也有几分惧他，因为他心里雪亮：这一回猩红石榴红若再从他指缝里逃走，同僚们是绝不会饶他的。

===Original===
The scheme by which he hoped to destroy not only the heroic leader but the entire League by bringing opprobrium and ridicule upon them, was wonderfully subtle in its refined cruelty, and Chauvelin, knowing by now something of Sir Percy Blakeney’s curiously blended character, was never for a moment in doubt but that he would write the infamous letter, save his wife by sacrificing his honour, and then seek oblivion and peace in suicide.

===Chinese===
他这一计，指望的不单是毁掉那位英雄首领，而是把耻辱和讥笑泼到他们全体头上，将整个同盟连根葬送；论其中精雕细琢的狠毒，实在妙到毫巅。而肖夫兰如今对珀西·布莱克尼爵士那副奇异的复合心性也已略知一二，因此他一刻也不曾怀疑：那人必定会写下那封无耻的信，以牺牲名节救出妻子，然后在自戕之中求取遗忘与安宁。

===Original===
With so much disgrace, so much mud cast upon their chief, the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel would cease to be. *That* had been Chauvelin’s plan all along. For this end he had schemed and thought and planned, from the moment that Robespierre had given him the opportunity of redeeming his failure of last year. He had built up the edifice of his intrigue, bit by bit, from the introduction of his tool, Candeille, to Marguerite at the Richmond gala, to the arrest of Lady Blakeney in Boulogne. All that remained for him to see now would be the attitude of Sir Percy Blakeney tonight, when, in exchange for the stipulated letter, he would see his wife set free.

===Chinese===
首领头上泼了这许多耻辱、这许多污泥，猩红石榴红同盟便只有散伙一途。*这*才是肖夫兰自始至终的盘算。为了这个结局，打从罗伯斯庇尔给他机会洗刷去年败绩那一刻起，他就一路谋划、思量、布置，把那座阴谋的大厦一砖一瓦垒了起来：从在里士满游园会上把他的工具康代伊引荐给玛格丽特起，到布莱克尼夫人在布洛涅落网止。如今他还要看的只有一件事：今晚珀西·布莱克尼爵士的态度——用那封讲定的信，换他亲眼看着妻子重获自由。

===Original===
All day Chauvelin had wondered how it would all go off. He had stage-managed everything, but he did not know how the chief actor would play his part.

===Chinese===
一整天，肖夫兰都在思忖这一切将如何了局。全局都是他一手调度，可主角将如何登场做戏，他却无从知晓。

===Original===
From time to time, when his feeling of restlessness became quite unendurable, the ex-ambassador would wander round Fort Gayole and on some pretext or other demand to see one or the other of his prisoners. Marguerite, however, observed complete silence in his presence: she acknowledged his greeting with a slight inclination of the head, and in reply to certain perfunctory queries of his﻿—which he put to her in order to justify his appearance﻿—she either nodded or gave curt monosyllabic answers through partially closed lips.

===Chinese===
那位前大使时不时地，熬不住心头焦躁，便绕着加约勒堡垒转悠，寻着这样那样的由头，要求见一见这名或那名囚犯。玛格丽特却在他面前始终缄默：他致意，她微微颔首；他提出几句敷衍的问话——那不过替他露面找个名目——她要么点头，要么半抿着嘴唇，吐出一两个字的生硬回答。

===Original===
“I trust that everything is arranged for your comfort, Lady Blakeney.”

“I thank you, sir.”

“You will be rejoining the *Daydream* tonight. Can I send a messenger over to the yacht for you?”

“I thank you. No.”

“Sir Percy is well. He is fast asleep, and hath not asked for your ladyship. Shall I let him know that you are well?”

===Chinese===
「但愿一切都替夫人安排得舒适，布莱克尼夫人。」

「多谢您，先生。」

「夫人今夜便能重回『白日梦号』了。可要我派个人到游艇上替您传话？」

「多谢。不必。」

「珀西爵士安好。他睡得正酣，不曾问起夫人。可要让他知道您也安好？」

===Original===
A nod of acquiescence from Marguerite and Chauvelin’s string of queries was at an end. He marvelled at her quietude and thought that she should have been as restless as himself.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特默默点头应允，肖夫兰这一串问话便算到了头。她的安静令他暗暗称奇，心想她本该同自己一样焦灼难安才对。

===Original===
Later on in the day, and egged on by Collot d’Herbois and by his own fears, he had caused Marguerite to be removed from No. 6.

This change he heralded by another brief visit to her, and his attitude this time was one of deferential apology.

===Chinese===
当天晚些时候，被科洛·德埃尔布瓦撺掇着，又加上自家的疑惧，他下令把玛格丽特从六号牢房挪了出去。

挪动之前，他又去看了她一眼；这一回，他的姿态是恭敬的告罪。

===Original===
“A matter of expediency, Lady Blakeney,” he explained, “and I trust that the change will be for your comfort.”

Again the same curt nod of acquiescence on her part, and a brief:

“As you command, Monsieur!”

===Chinese===
「无非是权宜之计，布莱克尼夫人，」他解释道，「还望这一挪动能让夫人更安逸些。」

她又是那样生硬地颔首默许，外加简短一句：

「悉听尊命，先生！」

===Original===
But when he had gone, she turned with a sudden passionate outburst towards the Abbé Foucquet, her faithful companion through the past long, weary hours. She fell on her knees beside him and sobbed in an agony of grief.

“Oh! if I could only know﻿ ﻿… if I could only see him!﻿ ﻿… for a minute﻿ ﻿… a second!﻿ ﻿… if I could only know!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
可他一走，她便猛地转身，感情的狂澜霎时决口，扑向富凯神父——这许多漫长难熬的时辰里一直忠心陪着她的人。她跪倒在他身旁，悲恸欲绝地呜咽起来。

「啊！我若能知道就好……我若能见他一面！……一分钟……一秒钟！……我若能知道就好！……」

===Original===
She felt as if the awful uncertainty would drive her mad.

If she could only know! If she could only know what he meant to do.

===Chinese===
她只觉得这可怕的悬而未决要把她逼疯了。

她若能知道该多好！她若能知道他打算怎么做该多好。

===Original===
“The good God knows!” said the old man, with his usual simple philosophy, “and perhaps it is all for the best.”

===Chinese===
「仁慈的天主自有数！」老人说，带着他一贯朴素的达观，「兴许这样倒是最妥当的。」

===Original===
The room which Chauvelin had now destined for Marguerite was one which gave from the larger one, wherein last night he had had his momentous interview with her and with Sir Percy.

It was small, square and dark, with no window in it: only a small ventilating hole high up in the wall and heavily grated. Chauvelin, who desired to prove to her that there was no wish on his part to add physical discomfort to her mental tortures, had given orders that the little place should be made as habitable as possible. A thick, soft carpet had been laid on the ground; there was an easy chair and a comfortable-looking couch with a couple of pillows and a rug upon it, and oh, marvel! on the round central table, a vase with a huge bunch of many-coloured dahlias which seemed to throw a note as if of gladness into this strange and gloomy little room.

===Chinese===
肖夫兰如今拨给玛格丽特的那间房，就从那间大屋里开门——昨夜，他正是在那大屋里同她、同珀西爵士进行了那场非同小可的会谈。

新房很小，四四方方，黑黢黢的，没有窗：只有墙高处一个小小的通气孔，装着粗重的铁栅。肖夫兰有意向她表明，他并不想在她精神的酷刑之外再添皮肉的苦楚，便下令把这方寸之地收拾得尽量可居。地上铺了厚软的地毯；有一张安乐椅，一张看着便舒适的卧榻，榻上搁着两三只靠枕、一方毯子；更奇的是，屋子正中的圆桌上，一只花瓶插着好大一束五彩缤纷的大丽花，仿佛给这间古怪阴郁的小屋投进了一抹欢愉的气息。

===Original===
At the furthest corner, too, a construction of iron uprights and crossway bars had been hastily contrived and fitted with curtains, forming a small recess, behind which was a tidy washstand, fine clean towels and plenty of fresh water. Evidently the shops of Boulogne had been commandeered in order to render Marguerite’s sojourn here outwardly agreeable.

===Chinese===
最里面的角落里，还有人草草搭起一座铁架，立柱横档一应俱全，挂上帘幔，围出一个小小的凹间；帘后是一张整洁的盥洗台、几条精致干净的毛巾和充足的清水。显见得布洛涅的各家铺子都被征用了，好叫玛格丽特此番居留，外表上过得安逸。

===Original===
But as the place was innocent of window, so was it innocent of doors. The one that gave into the large room had been taken out of its hinges, leaving only the frame, on each side of which stood a man from the municipal guard with fixed bayonet.

===Chinese===
只是这屋子既然没有窗，也同样没有门。通向大屋的那一扇，已从合页上卸了下来，只剩个门框，框子两侧各站着一个城防军士兵，枪上雪亮地插着刺刀。

===Original===
Chauvelin himself had conducted Marguerite to her new prison. She followed him﻿—silent and apathetic﻿—with not a trace of that awful torrent of emotion which had overwhelmed her but half-an-hour ago when she had fallen on her knees beside the old priest and sobbed her heart out in a passionate fit of weeping. Even the sight of the soldiers left her outwardly indifferent. As she stepped across the threshold she noticed that the door itself had been taken away: then she gave another quick glance at the soldiers, whose presence there would control her every movement.

===Chinese===
亲自引玛格丽特到这间新牢房来的，正是肖夫兰本人。她跟在他身后——沉默而木然——半个钟头前把她整个吞没的那股可怕的感情狂澜，此刻不留一丝痕迹；那时她跪在老神父身旁，痛哭失声，把心都哭了出来。便是望见那些士兵，她表面上也无动于衷。跨过门槛时，她注意到那扇门本身已被卸走：随即又飞快地瞥了士兵们一眼——有他们在场，她的一举一动都在管制之下了。

===Original===
The thought of Queen Marie Antoinette in the Conciergerie prison with the daily, hourly humiliation and shame which this constant watch imposed upon her womanly pride and modesty, flashed suddenly across Marguerite’s mind, and a deep blush of horror rapidly suffused her pale cheeks, whilst an almost imperceptible shudder shook her delicate frame.

===Chinese===
玛丽·安托瓦内特王后在古监狱里的景象，猛然掠过玛格丽特的心头——那种昼夜不离的看守，加在她女性的骄傲与廉耻之上、每日每时的羞辱和耻辱——一股骇然的红晕霎时漫上她苍白的双颊，一阵几乎察觉不出的战栗，震颤了她纤弱的身躯。

===Original===
Perhaps, as in a flash, she had at this moment received an inkling of what the nature of that terrible “either﻿—or” might be, with which Chauvelin was trying to force an English gentleman to dishonour. Sir Percy Blakeney’s wife had been threatened with Marie Antoinette’s fate.

===Chinese===
也许就在电光石火的这一瞬，她隐约窥见了那个可怕的「二者择一」究竟成色为何——肖夫兰正拿它胁迫一位英国绅士自甘蒙羞。珀西·布莱克尼爵士的妻子，已被拿玛丽·安托瓦内特的命运作过威胁了。

===Original===
“You see, Madame,” said her cruel enemy’s unctuous voice close to her ear, “that we have tried our humble best to make your brief sojourn here as agreeable as possible. May I express a hope that you will be quite comfortable in this room, until the time when Sir Percy will be ready to accompany you to the *Daydream*.”

“I thank you, sir,” she replied quietly.

“And if there is anything you require, I pray you to call. I shall be in the next room all day and entirely at your service.”

===Chinese===
「夫人请看，」她那狠毒的仇人油腔滑调的嗓音在她耳边响起，「我们已尽了绵薄之力，让夫人此番短暂居留尽量舒适。容我指望一句：在这间屋子里，直到珀西爵士准备停当、陪夫人登上『白日梦号』之前，夫人都能住得安逸。」

「多谢您，先生。」她平静地答道。

「夫人若有所需，敬请唤我。我整日就在隔壁屋里，专诚听候差遣。」

===Original===
A young orderly now entered bearing a small collation﻿—eggs, bread, milk and wine﻿—which he set on the central table. Chauvelin bowed low before Marguerite and withdrew. Anon he ordered the two sentinels to stand the other side of the doorway, against the wall of his own room, and well out of sight of Marguerite, so that, as she moved about her own narrow prison, if she ate or slept, she might have the illusion that she was unwatched.

===Chinese===
一名年轻勤务兵这时走了进来，端着一小桌茶点——鸡蛋、面包、牛奶和酒——摆在那张正中的圆桌上。肖夫兰向玛格丽特深深一躬，退了出去。随即他吩咐那两名哨兵站到门洞另一侧、靠着他自己那间屋的墙根，让玛格丽特瞧不见他们；这样，她在自己这方寸牢笼里或走动、或进食、或安睡，都可存一份无人监视的错觉。

===Original===
The sight of the soldiers had had the desired effect on her. Chauvelin had seen her shudder and knew that she understood or that she guessed. He was now satisfied and really had no wish to harass her beyond endurance.

===Chinese===
士兵那一眼在她身上收到了预期的效果。肖夫兰看见了她的战栗，知道她懂了，或是猜着了。他如今心满意足，实在也无意再把她逼到超出忍耐的绝地。

===Original===
Moreover, there was always the proclamation which threatened the breadwinners of Boulogne with death if Marguerite Blakeney escaped, and which would be in full force until Sir Percy had written, signed and delivered into Chauvelin’s hands the letter which was to be the signal for the general amnesty.

===Chinese===
何况还有那纸公告在：玛格丽特·布莱克尼若越狱而逃，布洛涅一城养家糊口之人一律处死。这道公告将一直有效，直到珀西爵士亲笔写下那封信、签上名、交到肖夫兰手里——那封信，便是全面大赦的号令。

===Original===
Chauvelin had indeed cause to be satisfied with his measures. There was no fear that his prisoners would attempt to escape.

===Chinese===
肖夫兰的种种布置，的确尽可自得。他手下的囚犯若想越狱，那是不必担忧的。

===Original===
Even Collot d’Herbois had to admit everything was well done. He had read the draft of the proposed letter and was satisfied with its contents. Gradually now into his loutish brain there had filtrated the conviction that Citizen Chauvelin was right, that that accursed Scarlet Pimpernel and his brood of English spies would be more effectually annihilated by all the dishonour and ridicule which such a letter written by the mysterious hero would heap upon them all, than they could ever be through the relentless work of the guillotine. His only anxiety now was whether the Englishman would write that letter.

===Chinese===
连科洛·德埃尔布瓦也不得不承认，桩桩件件都办得妥帖。那封拟议中信件的底稿他读过，对其内容颇为满意。渐渐地，他那个粗汉脑瓜里也渗进了一个信服：公民肖夫兰是对的——那该死的猩红石榴红和他那一窝英国奸细，与其靠断头台无休止的劳作去剿灭，倒不如靠这位神秘英雄亲笔写下这样一封信、把耻辱和讥笑堆到他们全体头上来消灭，来得更加斩尽杀绝。如今他唯一悬心的是：那英国人肯不肯写那封信。

===Original===
“Bah! he’ll do it,” he would say whenever he thought the whole matter over: “*Sacré tonnerre!* but ’tis an easy means to save his own skin.”

===Chinese===
「嘿！他会写的。」每回把整桩事从头盘算一遍，他总要这么说：「天打雷劈！（*Sacré tonnerre!*）这可是保住自家皮肉的省事法子。」

===Original===
“You would sign such a letter without hesitation, eh, Citizen Collot,” said Chauvelin, with well-concealed sarcasm, on one occasion when his colleague discussed the all-absorbing topic with him; “you would show no hesitation, if your life were at stake, and you were given the choice between writing that letter and﻿ ﻿… the guillotine?”

===Chinese===
「这样的信，您会毫不迟疑地签，呃，科洛公民？」有一回同僚同他谈起这个人人牵肠挂肚的话题时，肖夫兰用掩藏得极好的讥诮说道，「若是性命悬于一线，叫您在写这封信和……断头台之间拣一条路，您是断不会迟疑的，对不对？」

===Original===
“*Parbleu!*” responded Collot with conviction.

“More especially,” continued Chauvelin drily, “if a million francs were promised you as well?”

===Chinese===
「那还用说！（*Parbleu!*）」科洛斩钉截铁地应道。

「那就更不必说了，」肖夫兰冷冷地接下去，「倘或另外还许给您一百万法郎呢？」

===Original===
“*Sacré Anglais!*” swore Collot angrily, “you don’t propose giving him that money, do you?”

“We’ll place it ready to his hand, at any rate, so that it should appear as if he had actually taken it.”

===Chinese===
「该死的英国鬼！（*Sacré Anglais!*）」科洛怒冲冲地咒骂，「您总不是当真要把那笔钱给他吧？」

「无论如何，我们会把它放到他手边，好教事情看上去就像他当真收下了似的。」

===Original===
Collot looked up at his colleague in ungrudging admiration. Chauvelin had indeed left nothing undone, had thought everything out in this strangely conceived scheme for the destruction of the enemy of France.

===Chinese===
科洛抬起头望着他的同僚，佩服得毫无保留。在这桩奇想天开、专为毁灭法兰西仇敌的大计里，肖夫兰当真无所不办、无所不虑。

===Original===
“But in the name of all the dwellers in hell, Citizen,” admonished Collot, “guard that letter well, once it is in your hands.”

“I’ll do better than that,” said Chauvelin, “I will hand it over to you, Citizen Collot, and you shall ride with it to Paris at once.”

===Chinese===
「不过，看在地狱里众亡灵的份上，公民，」科洛告诫道，「那封信一旦到了您手，可要看牢了。」

「我有更好的办法，」肖夫兰说，「我会把它交到您手上，科洛公民，由您即刻带着它驰赴巴黎。」

===Original===
“Tonight!” assented Collot with a shout of triumph, as he brought his grimy fist crashing down on the table, “I’ll have a horse ready saddled at this very gate, and an escort of mounted men﻿ ﻿… we’ll ride like hell’s own furies and not pause to breathe until that letter is in Citizen Robespierre’s hands.”

===Chinese===
「今夜便去！」科洛一声得胜的大吼应道，那只乌黑油腻的拳头砰然砸在桌上，「我这就叫人在此门内备好鞍马，再点一队骑兵……咱们纵马疾驰，赛过地狱的复仇怨鬼，一口气也不歇，直跑到那封信进了罗伯斯庇尔公民之手。」

===Original===
“Well thought of, Citizen,” said Chauvelin approvingly. “I pray you give the necessary orders, that the horses be ready saddled, and the men booted and spurred, and waiting at the Gayole gate, at seven o’clock this evening.”

===Chinese===
「想得周到，公民。」肖夫兰嘉许道，「就请您下达必要的命令：鞍马备齐，人员登靴着刺，今晚七点在加约勒大门口待命。」

===Original===
“I wish the letter were written and safely in our hands by now.”

“Nay! the Englishman will have it ready by this evening, never fear. The tide is high at half-past seven, and he will be in haste for his wife to be aboard his yacht, ere the turn, even if he﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「但愿这会儿信已写好，稳稳落在咱们手里。」

「不会误事！那英国人今晚之前定会把它备好，尽管放心。七点半满潮，他急着要在转潮前送妻子登上自己的游艇呢，哪怕他……」

===Original===
He paused, savouring the thoughts which had suddenly flashed across his mind, and a look of intense hatred and cruel satisfaction for a moment chased away the studied impassiveness of his face.

“What do you mean, Citizen?” queried Collot anxiously, “even if he﻿ ﻿… what?﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
他顿住了，细细咂摸着心头骤然掠过的念头，一抹切齿的恨意和残忍的快意，一时间驱散了他脸上刻意养成的漠然。

「您这是什么意思，公民？」科洛焦急地追问，「哪怕他……怎么样？……」

===Original===
“Oh! nothing, nothing! I was only trying to make vague guesses as to what the Englishman will do *after* he has written the letter,” quoth Chauvelin reflectively.

“*Morbleu!* he’ll return to his own accursed country﻿ ﻿… glad enough to have escaped with his skin.﻿ ﻿… I suppose,” added Collot with sudden anxiety, “you have no fear that he will refuse at the last moment to write that letter?”

===Chinese===
「哦！没什么，没什么！我不过胡乱猜猜，那英国人把信*写完之后*还会做些什么。」肖夫兰若有所思地说。

「老天在上！（*Morbleu!*）他会滚回他那个天杀的国度去……能囫囵保住一张皮，就够他乐的了。……我琢磨着，」科洛忽然又焦虑起来，添了一句，「您就不怕他到最后一刻，忽然反悔，不肯写那封信？」

===Original===
The two men were sitting in the large room, out of which opened the one which was now occupied by Marguerite. They were talking at the further end of it, close to the window, and though Chauvelin had mostly spoken in a whisper, Collot had ofttimes shouted, and the ex-ambassador was wondering how much Marguerite had heard.

===Chinese===
这时两人正坐在那间大屋里，玛格丽特如今住的那间，便从这大屋里开门。他们坐在屋子最里头、紧挨窗口的地方说话；肖夫兰多半压着嗓音，科洛却时常扯开喉咙嚷，那位前大使正暗自纳罕：玛格丽特到底听去了多少。

===Original===
Now at Collot’s anxious query he gave a quick furtive glance in the direction of the further room wherein she sat, so silent and so still, that it seemed almost as if she must be sleeping.

===Chinese===
科洛这一焦急追问，他便朝那间里屋飞快地偷觑了一眼——她坐在里面，那样无声无息、纹丝不动，瞧上去简直像是睡着了。

===Original===
“You don’t think that the Englishman will refuse to write the letter?” insisted Collot with angry impatience.

“No!” replied Chauvelin quietly.

“But if he does?” persisted the other.

===Chinese===
「您当真以为那英国人不会拒写这封信？」科洛又急又恼，追着不放。

「不会！」肖夫兰平静地答道。

「可万一他不写呢？」对方仍不死心。

===Original===
“If he does, I send the woman to Paris tonight and have him hanged as a spy in this prison yard without further formality or trial﻿ ﻿…” replied Chauvelin firmly; “so either way, you see, Citizen,” he added in a whisper, “the Scarlet Pimpernel is done for.﻿ ﻿… But I think that he will write the letter.”

===Chinese===
「他若不写，我今夜就把那女人送往巴黎，再把他当奸细吊死在这监狱院子里，不必再行什么手续、开什么审——」肖夫兰斩钉截铁地答道，「所以您瞧，公民，横竖都一样，」他压低声音补上一句，「猩红石榴红是输定了。……不过，我想他会写那封信的。」

===Original===
“*Parbleu!* so do I!﻿ ﻿…” rejoined Collot with a coarse laugh.

===Chinese===
「那还用说！我也这么想！……」科洛粗声大笑，随声应道。

## XXXII. The Letter / XXXII. 那封信

===Original===
Later on, when his colleague left him in order to see to the horses and to his escort for tonight, Chauvelin called Sergeant Hébert, his old and trusted familiar, to him and gave him some final orders.

“The Angelus must be rung at the proper hour, friend Hébert,” he began with a grim smile.

“The Angelus, Citizen?” quoth the Sergeant, with complete stupefaction, “ ’tis months now since it has been rung. It was forbidden by a decree of the Convention, and I doubt me if any of our men would know how to set about it.”

Chauvelin’s eyes were fixed before him in apparent vacancy, while the same grim smile still hovered round his thin lips. Something of that irresponsible spirit of adventure which was the mainspring of all Sir Percy Blakeney’s actions, must for the moment have pervaded the mind of his deadly enemy.

===Chinese===
后来，等到同僚离开他、去张罗今晚的马匹和护卫之后，肖夫兰把自己素来倚重的心腹老部下埃贝尔（Hébert）中士唤到跟前，下了几道最后的命令。

「晚祷钟必须按时敲响，埃贝尔朋友，」他开口说道，唇边挂着一丝狞笑。

「晚祷钟，公民？」中士满脸的错愕，全然一副目瞪口呆的样子，「这钟已有好几个月没敲过了。国民公会一纸法令明令禁止，我看咱们的人里头，怕是没有一个晓得该怎么下手敲。」

肖夫兰的目光直望前方，仿佛一片茫然，那丝狞笑却依然在他的薄唇边游移。珀西·布莱克尼爵士一切行动的原动力，原是那种百无顾忌的冒险精神；此刻，这精神的几分气息，想必也侵入了这个死敌的头脑。

===Original===
Chauvelin had thought out this idea of having the Angelus rung tonight, and was thoroughly pleased with the notion. This was the day when the duel was to have been fought; seven o’clock would have been the very hour, and the sound of the Angelus to have been the signal for combat, and there was something very satisfying in the thought, that that same Angelus should be rung, as a signal that the Scarlet Pimpernel was withered and broken at last.

In answer to Hébert’s look of bewilderment Chauvelin said quietly:

“We must have some signal between ourselves and the guard at the different gates, also with the harbour officials: at a given moment the general amnesty must take effect and the harbour become a free port. I have a fancy that the signal shall be the ringing of the Angelus: the cannons at the gates and the harbour can boom in response; then the prisons can be thrown open and prisoners can either participate in the evening fête or leave the city immediately, as they choose. The Committee of Public Safety has promised the amnesty: it will carry out its promise to the full, and when Citizen Collot d’Herbois arrives in Paris with the joyful news, all natives of Boulogne in the prisons there will participate in the free pardon too.”

“I understand all that, Citizen,” said Hébert, still somewhat bewildered, “but not the Angelus.”

“A fancy, friend Hébert, and I mean to have it.”

===Chinese===
今晚敲响晚祷钟的主意，是肖夫兰自己琢磨出来的，他对这个念头得意透了。这本该是决斗举行的日子；七点整正是交手的时辰，晚祷钟声本该是开战的信号。而如今，还是这一记晚祷钟将被敲响，作为猩红石榴红终于枯萎、终于折戟的信号——这念头里，自有一种叫人心满意足的东西。

埃贝尔一脸茫然地望着他，肖夫兰便轻声说道：

「咱们与各处城门的守卫、还有港务官员之间，总得有个联络信号：一到指定时刻，全面大赦就要生效，海港就要成为自由港。我忽发奇想，要让这信号就是敲响晚祷钟：各城门和港口的大炮可以齐声回应；然后监狱敞开大门，囚犯们愿意参加晚间庆典也好，愿意即刻出城也好，悉听尊便。公共安全委员会既然许下了大赦，就会把承诺兑现到十足；等科洛·德埃尔布瓦公民带着这喜讯抵达巴黎，关押在那里的布洛涅人也一样能享受这场自由赦免。」

「这些我都明白，公民，」埃贝尔说，仍旧有些糊涂，「可这晚祷钟，我闹不明白。」

「一时兴起罢了，埃贝尔朋友，可我偏要这么办。」

===Original===
“But who is to ring it, Citizen?”

“*Morbleu!* haven’t you one *calotin* left in Boulogne whom you can press into doing this service?”

“Aye! *calotins* enough! there’s the Abbé Foucquet in this very building﻿ ﻿… in No. 6 cell﻿ ﻿…”

“*Sacré tonnerre!*” ejaculated Chauvelin exultingly, “the very man! I know his dossier well! Once he is free, he will make straightway for England﻿ ﻿… he and his family﻿ ﻿… and will help to spread the glorious news of the dishonour and disgrace of the much-vaunted Scarlet Pimpernel!﻿ ﻿… The very man, friend Hébert!﻿ ﻿… Let him be stationed here﻿ ﻿… to see the letter written﻿ ﻿… to see the money handed over﻿—for we will go through with that farce﻿—and make him understand that the moment I give him the order, he can run over to his old church St. Joseph and ring the Angelus.﻿ ﻿… The old fool will be delighted﻿ ﻿… more especially when he knows that he will thereby be giving the very signal which will set his own sister’s children free.﻿ ﻿… You understand?﻿ ﻿…”

“I understand, Citizen.”

===Chinese===
「可派谁去敲呢，公民？」

「见鬼！（Morbleu!）你们布洛涅就剩不下一个*calotin*（教士佬），好逼他来当这份差么？」

「有！*calotin*（教士佬）有的是！富凯神父不就在这栋楼里……就在六号牢房……」

「该死的天雷！（Sacré tonnerre!）」肖夫兰扬扬得意地脱口喊道，「正是这个人！他的案卷我了如指掌！他一得自由，就会径直投奔英格兰去……带着他一家子……还会帮着把那大肆吹擂的猩红石榴红身败名裂的光荣消息四处传扬！……就是这个人，埃贝尔朋友！……把他安顿在这里……亲眼看那封信写就……亲眼看着钱款交割——这出戏咱们是要做全套的——再让他明白，我一声令下，他就可以撒腿跑回他的老教堂圣约瑟夫，去敲响晚祷钟。……这老傻子准会乐开了花……尤其等他知道，他亲手敲响的，正是放他自己妹妹的孩子们自由的信号。……你明白了么？……」

「明白了，公民。」

===Original===
“And you can make the old *calotin* understand?”

“I think so, Citizen.﻿ ﻿… You want him in this room.﻿ ﻿… At what time?”

“A quarter before seven.”

“Yes. I’ll bring him along myself, and stand over him, lest he play any pranks.”

===Chinese===
「那你能叫那老*calotin*（教士佬）明白么？」

「我想可以，公民。……您要他到这间屋里来。……几点钟？」

「七点差一刻。」

「是。我亲自带他过来，寸步不离地盯着他，免得他耍什么花招。」

===Original===
“Oh! he’ll not trouble you,” sneered Chauvelin, “he’ll be deeply interested in the proceedings. The woman will be here too, remember,” he added with a jerky movement of the hand in the direction of Marguerite’s room, “the two might be made to stand together, with four of your fellows round them.”

“I understand, Citizen. Are any of us to escort the Citizen Foucquet when he goes to St. Joseph?”

“Aye! two men had best go with him. There will be a crowd in the streets by then﻿ ﻿… How far is it from here to the church?”

===Chinese===
「哦！他不会给您添乱的，」肖夫兰冷笑道，「他只会看得津津有味。那女人也会到场，记住，」他朝玛格丽特牢房的方向猛地一挥手，又添了一句，「不妨叫这两人站在一处，四周派上你四个弟兄看着。」

「明白了，公民。富凯公民去圣约瑟夫教堂时，要不要咱们的人护送？」

「要！顶好派两个人跟着他。到时候街上人少不了……从这里到教堂有多远？」

===Original===
“Less than five minutes.”

“Good. See to it that the doors are opened and the bell ropes easy of access.”

“It shall be seen to, Citizen. How many men will you have inside this room tonight?”

===Chinese===
「不到五分钟。」

「好。务必把门户都敞开，钟绳也搭在顺手可及的地方。」

「一定办妥，公民。今晚这间屋里，您要布置多少人？」

===Original===
“Let the walls be lined with men whom you can trust. I anticipate neither trouble nor resistance. The whole thing is a simple formality to which the Englishman has already intimated his readiness to submit. If he changes his mind at the last moment there will be no Angelus rung, no booming of the cannons or opening of the prison doors: there will be no amnesty, and no free pardon. The woman will be at once conveyed to Paris, and﻿ ﻿… But he’ll not change his mind, friend Hébert,” he concluded in suddenly altered tones, and speaking quite lightly, “he’ll not change his mind.”

The conversation between Chauvelin and his familiar had been carried on in whispers: not that the Terrorist cared whether Marguerite overheard or not, but whispering had become a habit with this man, whose tortuous ways and subtle intrigues did not lend themselves to discussion in a loud voice.

===Chinese===
「叫墙边站满你信得过的人。我既不预计有麻烦，也不预计有抵抗。整件事不过走一道简单的过场，那英国人已经示意他甘愿照办。倘若他最后关头变了卦，那就没有晚祷钟可敲，没有大炮轰鸣、监狱门洞开：没有大赦，也没有自由赦免。那女人会被即刻押往巴黎，而且……不过他不会变卦的，埃贝尔朋友，」他结语时声调陡然一变，说得轻描淡写，「他不会变卦的。」

肖夫兰与他心腹之间的这番对谈，自始至终是咬着耳朵进行的：倒不是这恐怖党人在乎玛格丽特听见与否，只是耳语早已成了此人的积习——他那曲曲折折的门道、幽微隐秘的阴谋，本就不宜高声谈论。

===Original===
Chauvelin was sitting at the central table, just where he had been last night when Sir Percy Blakeney’s sudden advent broke in on his meditations. The table had been cleared of the litter of multitudinous papers which had encumbered it before. On it now there were only a couple of heavy pewter candlesticks, with the tallow candles fixed ready in them, a leather-pad, an inkwell, a sandbox, and two or three quill pens: everything disposed, in fact, for the writing and signing of the letter.

Already in imagination, Chauvelin saw his impudent enemy, the bold and daring adventurer, standing there beside that table and putting his name to the consummation of his own infamy. The mental picture thus evoked brought a gleam of cruel satisfaction and of satiated lust into the keen, ferret-like face, and a smile of intense joy lit up the narrow, pale-coloured eyes.

===Chinese===
肖夫兰坐在居中的那张桌旁，正是昨夜珀西·布莱克尼爵士突然降临、打断他冥想的那个位置。先前堆满桌面、碍手碍脚的乱纸已经清理一空。如今桌上只搁着一对沉甸甸的白镴烛台，牛油蜡烛都已插好待燃，另有皮垫一块、墨水壶一只、沙盒一方，还有两三支鹅毛笔：事实上一切都布置停当，专候那封信的书写与签署。

肖夫兰在想象中已经望见他那个厚颜无耻的仇敌、那个胆大包天的冒险家，站在桌旁落笔签名，给自己的奇耻大辱画上最后一笔。这一幅心造的图景，给他那张貂鼠般精明的脸注入一缕残忍的快意、一股餍足的贪欲，那双狭长的浅色眼睛里，燃起了狂喜的笑影。

===Original===
He looked round the room where the great scene would be enacted: two soldiers were standing guard outside Marguerite’s prison, two more at attention near the door which gave on the passage: his own half-dozen picked men were waiting his commands in the corridor. Presently the whole room would be lined with troops, himself and Collot standing with eyes fixed on the principal actor of the drama! Hébert with specially selected troopers standing on guard over Marguerite!

No, no! he had left nothing to chance this time, and down below the horses would be ready saddled, that were to convey Collot and the precious document to Paris.

No! nothing was left to chance, and in either case he was bound to win. Sir Percy Blakeney would either write the letter in order to save his wife, and heap dishonour on himself, or he would shrink from the terrible ordeal at the last moment and let Chauvelin and the Committee of Public Safety work their will with her and him.

“In that case the pillory as a spy and summary hanging for you, my friend,” concluded Chauvelin in his mind, “and for your wife﻿ ﻿… Bah, once you are out of the way, even she will cease to matter.”

===Chinese===
他环视这间即将上演重头戏的屋子：两名士兵守在玛格丽特的牢房门外，另有两名在通往走廊的门前肃立待命；他自己那六名精选的亲随正在走廊里听候差遣。转眼之间，整间屋子便要沿墙排满兵士，他和科洛将目光灼灼，盯住这出戏码的主角！埃贝尔则带着特意挑选的骑兵，看守着玛格丽特！

不，不！这一回他不留半点侥幸；楼下马匹也将鞍鞯备齐，专候载着科洛和那份珍贵文书奔往巴黎。

不！什么都不凭运气，而且无论哪一种结局，他都赢定了。珀西·布莱克尼爵士要么写下那封信，为救妻子而把耻辱堆到自己头上；要么就在最后关头畏缩却步，任凭肖夫兰和公共安全委员会对他夫妻二人为所欲为。

「那样的话，我的朋友，等着你的就是奸细罪名的示众枷，外加一场从速的绞刑，」肖夫兰在心里下了结论，「至于你的妻子……呸，等你这人一没了，连她也无足轻重了。」

===Original===
He left Hébert on guard in the room. An irresistible desire seized him to go and have a look at his discomfited enemy, and from the latter’s attitude make a shrewd guess as to what he meant to do tonight.

Sir Percy had been given a room on one of the upper floors of the old prison. He had in no way been closely guarded, and the room itself had been made as comfortable as may be. He had seemed quite happy and contented when he had been conducted hither by Chauvelin, the evening before.

===Chinese===
他留下埃贝尔在屋里守着。一阵按捺不住的欲念攫住了他：他要去看一看那个一败涂地的仇敌，并从他此刻的做派里，精明地猜一猜他今晚究竟作何打算。

珀西爵士被安置在老监狱顶上一层的一间屋里。看守并不甚严，屋子本身也收拾得尽可能舒坦。头天傍晚肖夫兰领他上这里来时，他看上去还挺快活、挺受用。

===Original===
“I hope you quite understand, Sir Percy, that you are my guest here tonight,” Chauvelin had said suavely, “and that you are free to come and go, just as you please.”

“Lud love you, sir,” Sir Percy had replied gaily, “but I verily believe that I am.”

“It is only Lady Blakeney whom we have cause to watch until tomorrow,” added Chauvelin with quiet significance. “Is that not so, Sir Percy?”

But Sir Percy seemed, whenever his wife’s name was mentioned, to lapse into irresistible somnolence. He yawned now with his usual affectation, and asked at what hour gentlemen in France were wont to breakfast.

===Chinese===
「希望您完全明白，珀西爵士，今晚您是我这里的贵客，」肖夫兰当时和颜悦色地说，「来去出入，悉听尊便。」

「老天爷疼您，先生，」珀西爵士欢欢喜喜地答道，「我当真相信我就是呢。」

「只是布莱克尼夫人，我们还有缘由要看管到明天，」肖夫兰补上一句，语气平静而意味深长，「可不是么，珀西爵士？」

可每逢提起妻子的名字，珀西爵士仿佛总要坠入一种不可抗拒的昏昏欲睡。此刻他又装腔作势地打了个呵欠，一如平素的做派，随即问起法兰西的先生们惯于几点钟用早餐。

===Original===
Since then Chauvelin had not seen him. He had repeatedly asked how the English prisoner was faring, and whether he seemed to be sleeping and eating heartily. The orderly in charge invariably reported that the Englishman seemed well, but did not eat much. On the other hand, he had ordered, and lavishly paid for, measure after measure of brandy and bottle after bottle of wine.

“Hm! how strange these Englishmen are!” mused Chauvelin; “this so-called hero is nothing but a wine-sodden brute, who seeks to nerve himself for a trying ordeal by drowning his faculties in brandy﻿ ﻿… Perhaps after all he doesn’t care!﻿ ﻿…”

But the wish to have a look at that strangely complex creature﻿—hero, adventurer or mere lucky fool﻿—was irresistible, and Chauvelin in the latter part of the afternoon went up to the room which had been allotted to Sir Percy Blakeney.

===Chinese===
打那以后，肖夫兰就没有再见过他。他一再打听那位英国囚徒的光景，问他睡得香不香、吃得好不好。负责照管的勤务兵回回都禀报说，英国人看着挺好，只是吃得不多。另一方面，他倒是白兰地一杯接一杯、葡萄酒一瓶接一瓶地点，付起钱来大手大脚。

「哼！这些英国人真是怪物！」肖夫兰暗自琢磨；「这个所谓的英雄，不过是一头泡在酒里的畜生，想靠白兰地把自己的神智淹死，好壮起胆来挨那场煎熬的考验……说不定他压根儿就不在乎！……」

可是，亲眼看看这个复杂得出奇的造物——英雄也罢，冒险家也罢，或者不过是个走运的蠢货也罢——这个愿望实在按捺不住。于是午后偏晚时分，肖夫兰登上分配给珀西·布莱克尼爵士的那间屋子。

===Original===
He never moved now without his escort, and this time also two of his favourite bodyguards accompanied him to the upper floor. He knocked at the door, but received no answer, and after a second or two he bade his men wait in the corridor and, gently turning the latch, walked in.

There was an odour of brandy in the air; on the table two or three empty bottles of wine and a glass half-filled with cognac testified to the truth of what the orderly had said, whilst sprawling across the camp bedstead, which obviously was too small for his long limbs, his head thrown back, his mouth open for a vigorous snore, lay the imperturbable Sir Percy, fast asleep.

===Chinese===
如今他出行必带护卫，这一回也是两名得力的贴身卫士随他上楼。他敲了敲门，无人应答；过了一两秒，他吩咐手下在走廊里候着，自己轻轻拧开门闩，走了进去。

空气里一股白兰地气味；桌上两三只空酒瓶和半杯斟着的干邑，印证了勤务兵所言不虚。而行军床——那床对他那双长腿而言显然太窄——上面大喇喇地横陈着泰然自若的珀西爵士，头向后仰，嘴巴大张，正酝酿着一记响亮的鼾声，睡得酣沉。

===Original===
Chauvelin went up to the bedstead and looked down upon the reclining figure of the man who had oft been called the most dangerous enemy of Republican France.

Of a truth, a fine figure of a man, Chauvelin was ready enough to admit that; the long, hard limbs, the wide chest, and slender, white hands all bespoke the man of birth, breeding and energy: the face, too, looked strong and clearly-cut in repose, now that the perpetually inane smile did not play round the firm lips, nor the lazy, indolent expression mar the seriousness of the straight brow. For one moment﻿—it was a mere flash﻿—Chauvelin felt almost sorry that so interesting a career should be thus ignominiously brought to a close.

The Terrorist felt that if his own future, his own honour and integrity were about to be so hopelessly crushed, he would have wandered up and down this narrow room like a caged beast, eating out his heart with self-reproach and remorse, and racking his nerves and brain for an issue out of the terrible alternative which meant dishonour or death.

===Chinese===
肖夫兰走到床前，低头端详这具横陈的躯体——此人常被称作法兰西共和国最危险的敌人。

说实在的，好一条汉子，这层肖夫兰倒乐于承认；修长结实的四肢，宽阔的胸膛，纤细白皙的双手，处处透出此人家世高贵、教养有素、精力过人：那张脸在安眠中亦显得刚毅而轮廓分明，只因那副永恒的傻笑此刻不再绕着坚毅的唇边打转，那副慵懒怠惰的神情也不再搅扰端正额头的庄重。有那么一瞬——仅仅一闪——肖夫兰几乎感到惋惜：如此多姿多彩的一生，竟要以这般不体面的方式收场。

这恐怖党人心想，倘若即将这样万劫不复地被碾碎的，是他自己的前程、他自己的名誉与操守，他必定会像一头困兽，在这间窄屋里来回踱步，让自责与悔恨啃啮内心，绞尽神经、榨干脑髓，也要从那非辱即死的绝境里寻出一条生路。

===Original===
But this man drank and slept.

“Perhaps he doesn’t care!”

And as if in answer to Chauvelin’s puzzled musing a deep snore escaped the sleeping adventurer’s parted lips.

===Chinese===
可这个人，只管喝酒睡觉。

「说不定他压根儿不在乎！」

仿佛是要回答肖夫兰这困惑的沉思似的，熟睡的冒险家张开的双唇间，迸出一记深沉的鼾声。

===Original===
Chauvelin sighed, perplexed and troubled. He looked round the little room, then went up to a small side table which stood against the wall and on which were two or three quill pens and an inkwell, also some loosely scattered sheets of paper. These he turned over with a careless hand and presently came across a closely written page:

“Citizen Chauvelin:﻿—In consideration of a further sum of one million francs﻿ ﻿…”

It was the beginning of the letter!﻿ ﻿… only a few words so far﻿ ﻿… with several corrections of misspelt words﻿ ﻿… and a line left out here and there which confused the meaning﻿ ﻿… a beginning made by the unsteady hand of that drunken fool﻿ ﻿… an attempt only at present.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
肖夫兰叹了口气，又是困惑，又是烦乱。他环视这间小屋，随后走到靠墙的一张小边桌前：桌上有两三支鹅毛笔、一只墨水壶，还松松散散摊着几页纸。他漫不经心地随手翻检，不一会儿便翻到一页写满字迹的纸：

「肖夫兰公民：——兹考虑到再支付一百万法郎之数……」

这就是那封信的开头！……眼下只落了几句……好几处拼错的字涂改过……这里漏一行、那里漏一行，文意都不相连贯……出自那醉鬼颤抖不稳的手笔的一个开端……眼下不过是试笔罢了。……

===Original===
But still﻿ ﻿… a beginning.

Close by was the draft of it as written out by Chauvelin, and which Sir Percy had evidently begun to copy.

He had made up his mind then.﻿ ﻿… He meant to subscribe with his own hand to his lasting dishonour﻿ ﻿… and meaning it, he slept!

Chauvelin felt the paper trembling in his hand. He felt strangely agitated and nervous, now that the issue was so near﻿ ﻿… so sure!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
但终究……是个开端。

旁边放着肖夫兰亲笔誊清的那份底稿，珀西爵士显然已经开始照抄了。

这么说，他是拿定主意了。……他打算亲笔署名，把自己钉上终身的耻辱……而一边这么打算着，一边竟还睡得着觉！

肖夫兰觉得那页纸在自己手里打颤。结局既已这般迫近……这般笃定！……他心头竟涌起一阵莫名的激动与紧张。

===Original===
“There’s no demmed hurry for that, is there﻿ ﻿… er﻿ ﻿… Monsieur Chaubertin?﻿ ﻿…” came from the slowly wakening Sir Percy in somewhat thick, heavy accents, accompanied by a prolonged yawn. “I haven’t got the demmed thing quite ready﻿ ﻿…”

Chauvelin had been so startled that the paper dropped from his hand. He stooped to pick it up.

“Nay! why should you be so scared, sir?” continued Sir Percy lazily, “did you think I was drunk?﻿ ﻿… I assure you, sir, on my honour, I am not so drunk as you think I am.”

===Chinese===
「那件事犯不着这么该死的着急，是不是……呃……肖贝尔坦先生？……」这话从徐徐醒转的珀西爵士口中传来，嗓音颇为含混滞重，还拖着一记长长的呵欠。「那该死的东西，我还没全弄妥当呢……」

肖夫兰猝不及防，那页纸竟从指间跌落。他俯身去拾。

「嗳！您何至于这样吃惊，先生？」珀西爵士懒洋洋地接着说，「您方才莫不是当真以为我醉了？……我向您担保，先生，凭我的名誉，我没有您想的那么醉。」

===Original===
“I have no doubt, Sir Percy,” replied Chauvelin ironically, “that you have all your marvellous faculties entirely at your command.﻿ ﻿… I must apologize for disturbing your papers,” he added, replacing the half-written page on the table, “I thought perhaps that if the letter was ready﻿ ﻿…”

“It will be, sir﻿ ﻿… it will be﻿ ﻿… for I am not drunk, I assure you﻿ ﻿… and can write with a steady hand﻿ ﻿… and do honour to my signature.﻿ ﻿…”

“When will you have the letter ready, Sir Percy?”

“The *Daydream* must leave the harbour at the turn of the tide,” quoth Sir Percy thickly. “It’ll be demmed well time by then﻿ ﻿… won’t it, sir?﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我毫不怀疑，珀西爵士，」肖夫兰反唇相讥，「您那些神乎其神本领，无一处不听您调遣。……冒昧翻扰了您的文稿，还请恕罪，」他一面把那半页信纸放回桌上，一面又道，「我心想，倘若信已经写好了……」

「会写好的，先生……会写好的……因为我没有醉，我向您担保……我能稳稳当当落笔……也不辱没我的签名。……」

「您几时能把信备妥，珀西爵士？」

「『白日梦号』得赶转潮离港，」珀西爵士口齿含混地说。「到那会儿，时候也就该到了……可不是么，先生？……」

===Original===
“About sundown, Sir Percy﻿ ﻿… not later﻿ ﻿…”

“About sundown﻿ ﻿… not later﻿ ﻿…” muttered Blakeney, as he once more stretched his long limbs along the narrow bed.

He gave a loud and hearty yawn.

“I’ll not fail you﻿ ﻿…” he murmured, as he closed his eyes, and gave a final struggle to get his head at a comfortable angle, “the letter will be written in my best cali﻿ ﻿… calig.﻿ ﻿… Lud! but I’m not so drunk as you think I am.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「大约日落时分，珀西爵士……不得再晚……」

「大约日落时分……不得再晚……」布莱克尼喃喃念着，又把那双长腿伸直在窄床上。

他打了个响亮而痛快的呵欠。

「误不了您的事……」他阖上眼睛，脑袋最后又挣了两下，好寻个舒服的角度，一边喃喃说道，「那封信，我要拿出我最漂亮的书……书法来写。老天！我可没有您想的那么醉。……」

===Original===
But as if to belie his own oft-repeated assertion, hardly was the last word out of his mouth than his stertorous and even breathing proclaimed the fact that he was once more fast asleep.

With a shrug of the shoulders and a look of unutterable contempt at his broken-down enemy, Chauvelin turned on his heel and went out of the room.

But outside in the corridor he called the orderly to him and gave strict commands that no more wine or brandy was to be served to the Englishman under any circumstances whatever.

“He has two hours in which to sleep off the effects of all that brandy which he had consumed,” he mused as he finally went back to his own quarters, “and by that time he will be able to write with a steady hand.”

===Chinese===
可仿佛偏要拆穿他自己翻来覆去的那句申明似的，最后一个字才出口，他那粗重匀停的鼾息便已昭告：他又一次沉沉睡去了。

肖夫兰朝这个垮塌了的仇敌投去难以言喻的轻蔑一瞥，耸了耸肩，转身出了屋子。

可到了外头的走廊上，他把勤务兵唤到跟前，严令无论出于何种情由，一概不得再给那英国人供应葡萄酒或白兰地。

「他灌下去的那些白兰地，还有两个钟头好让他睡醒，」他终于回自己住处去时，心里盘算着，「到那会儿，他就能稳稳当当地写字了。」

## XXXIII / 英国间谍
===Original===

The English Spy

And now at last the shades of evening were drawing in thick and fast. Within the walls of Fort Gayole the last rays of the setting sun had long ago ceased to shed their dying radiance, and through the thick stone embrasures and the dusty panes of glass the grey light of dusk soon failed to penetrate.

In the large ground-floor room with its window opened upon the wide promenade of the southern ramparts, a silence reigned which was oppressive. The air was heavy with the fumes of the two tallow candles on the table, which smoked persistently.

Against the walls a row of figures in dark blue uniforms with scarlet facings, drab breeches and heavy riding boots, silent and immovable, with fixed bayonets like so many automatons lining the room all round; at some little distance from the central table and out of the immediate circle of light, a small group composed of five soldiers in the same blue and scarlet uniforms. One of these was Sergeant Hébert. In the centre of this group two persons were sitting: a woman and an old man.

The Abbé Foucquet had been brought down from his prison cell a few minutes ago, and told to watch what would go on around him, after which he would be allowed to go to his old church of St. Joseph and ring the Angelus once more before he and his family left Boulogne forever.

The Angelus would be the signal for the opening of all the prison gates in the town. Everyone tonight could come and go as they pleased, and having rung the Angelus, the abbé would be at liberty to join François and Félicité and their old mother, his sister, outside the purlieus of the town.

The Abbé Foucquet did not quite understand all this, which was very rapidly and roughly explained to him. It was such a very little while ago that he had expected to see the innocent children mounting up those awful steps which lead to the guillotine, whilst he himself was looking death quite near in the face, that all this talk of amnesty and of pardon had not quite fully reached his brain.

===Chinese===

英国间谍

夜色终于又浓又疾地四合拢来。加约勒堡垒的墙垣之内，落日最后的余晖早已敛尽了它垂死的光华，不多时，连暮色灰蒙蒙的光也透不进那厚厚的石砌炮眼与蒙尘的窗玻璃了。

底层的大屋里，窗子敞开着，正对南面城墙上宽阔的漫步道，一片压抑人的寂静笼罩全屋。桌上两支牛油蜡烛兀自冒着烟，空气里弥漫着呛人的浊气。

沿墙立着一列人影：深蓝的军装缀着猩红镶边，土黄的马裤，沉重的马靴，个个缄默不动，刺刀平端，恰似一具具自动人偶，把屋子环了一周；离中央的桌子稍远、在烛光的直接光圈之外，聚着一小队士兵，还是那身蓝红制服，共五人，其中一个便是埃贝尔中士。这队人正中坐着两个人：一个女人，一个老人。

富凯神父几分钟前刚被人从囚室里带下来，有人告诉他，要他看着眼下在他周围将要发生的一切；事毕之后，准他回到他的老教堂圣约瑟夫，再敲一回晚祷钟（Angelus），然后他就携同家人，永远离开布洛涅。

晚祷钟一响，便是全城监狱大门齐开的信号。今夜无论什么人，都可以随意来去；敲过晚祷钟，老神父便尽可以出城，到城厢之外同弗朗索瓦、费利西泰以及他们的老母亲——他自己的妹妹——会合。

这番话向他交代得又急又粗，富凯神父听得并不真切。分明才过了片刻，他还眼见着要瞧那几个无辜的孩子登上通往断头台的骇人台阶，他自己也正与死亡打个照面；此刻这一场大赦呀、赦免呀的言语，竟还没有真正进到他脑子里去。

===Original===

But he was quite content that it had all been ordained by *le bon Dieu*, and very happy at the thought of ringing the dearly-loved Angelus in his own old church once again. So when he was peremptorily pushed into the room and found himself close to Marguerite, with four or five soldiers standing round them, he quietly pulled his old rosary from his pocket and began murmuring gentle “Paters” and “Aves” under his breath.

Beside him sat Marguerite, rigid as a statue: her cloak thrown over her shoulders, so that its hood might hide her face. She could not now have said how that awful day had passed, how she had managed to survive the terrible, nerve-racking suspense, the agonizing doubt as to what was going to happen. But above all, what she had found most unendurable was the torturing thought that in this same grim and frowning building her husband was there﻿ ﻿… somewhere﻿ ﻿… how far or how near she could not say﻿ ﻿… but she knew that she was parted from him and perhaps would not see him again, not even at the hour of death.

That Percy would never write that infamous letter and *live*, she knew. That he might write it in order to save her she feared was possible, whilst the look of triumph on Chauvelin’s face had aroused her most agonizing terrors.

When she was summarily ordered to go into the next room, she realized at once that all hope now was more than futile. The walls lined with troops, the attitude of her enemies, and above all that table with paper, ink, and pens, ready, as it were, for the accomplishment of the hideous and monstrous deed, all made her very heart numb, as if it were held within the chill embrace of death.

“If the woman moves, speaks or screams, gag her at once!” said Collot roughly the moment she sat down, and Sergeant Hébert stood over her, gag and cloth in hand, whilst two soldiers placed heavy hands on her shoulders.

But she neither moved, nor spoke, not even presently when a loud and cheerful voice came echoing from a distant corridor, and anon the door opened and her husband came in, accompanied by Chauvelin.

===Chinese===

不过，这一切既都是*好上帝*（le bon Dieu）安排下的，他便满心安然；一想到又能亲手在自己的老教堂里敲响那心爱的晚祷钟，他更觉欢喜不尽。因此，当他被不由分说地推进屋来、发觉自己紧挨着玛格丽特、身边围着四五个士兵时，他只安安静静地从口袋里摸出那串旧念珠，开始低声默诵轻柔的《天主经》和《圣母经》。

他身旁坐着玛格丽特，僵直得像一尊雕像：斗篷披在肩头，好教风帽遮住她的脸。这骇人的一天是怎么挨过去的；那折磨神经的悬望、那不知下一刻要发生什么的噬心疑惧，她又是怎么挺过来的——如今她已说不上来了。而最最难捱的，还是一个煎熬人的念头：就在这座阴森逼人的同一座堡垒里，她的丈夫就在那里……在某个地方……多远多近，她说不上……可她知道自己同他已被隔开，或许再不能相见，连临死的那一刻也不能。

珀西决计不会写了那封无耻的信还*活着*，这一点她知道。而为了救她，他或许当真会写下那封信——她唯恐这是可能的；肖夫兰脸上那一副得胜的神气，更勾起了她最深切的恐惧。

待到她被喝令进隔壁那间屋子，她立刻明白，此刻一切指望都不止是徒然。沿墙列队的士兵、仇敌们的种种神情姿态，尤其是那张摆着纸、墨水和笔的桌子——仿佛专候着去干成那桩骇人听闻的滔天罪孽——这一切使她的心整个麻木了，仿佛被死亡冰冷的怀抱攥住了一般。

「这女人要是动一动、开一声口、喊一嗓子，马上塞住她的嘴！」她刚坐下，科洛便粗声喝道；埃贝尔中士手里拿着塞口的布巾立在她身后，两个士兵则把沉重的手掌按上她的肩头。

可她既没有动，也没有出声；过后不久，远处走廊里传来一个洪亮快活的声音，回声荡漾，接着门开了，她的丈夫由肖夫兰陪着走进屋来——便是这时，她仍旧一动不动，一言不发。

===Original===

The ex-ambassador was very obviously in a state of acute nervous tension; his hands were tightly clasped behind his back, and his movements were curiously irresponsible and jerky. But Sir Percy Blakeney looked a picture of calm unconcern: the lace bow at his throat was tied with scrupulous care, his eyeglass upheld at quite the correct angle, and his delicate-coloured caped coat was thrown back just sufficiently to afford a glimpse of the dainty cloth suit and exquisitely embroidered waistcoat beneath.

He was the perfect presentation of a London dandy, and might have been entering a royal drawing-room in company with an honoured guest. Marguerite’s eyes were riveted on him as he came well within the circle of light projected by the candles, but not even with that acute sixth sense of a passionate and loving woman could she detect the slightest tremor in the aristocratic hands which held the gold-rimmed eyeglass, nor the faintest quiver of the firmly moulded lips.

This had occurred just as the bell of the old Beffroi chimed three-quarters after six. Now it was close on seven, and in the centre of the room and with his face and figure well lighted up by the candles, at the table pen in hand sat Sir Percy writing.

At his elbow just behind him stood Chauvelin on the one side and Collot d’Herbois on the other, both watching with fixed and burning eyes the writing of that letter.

Sir Percy seemed in no hurry. He wrote slowly and deliberately, carefully copying the draft of the letter which was propped up in front of him. The spelling of some of the French words seemed to have troubled him at first, for when he began he made many facetious and self-deprecatory remarks anent his own want of education, and carelessness in youth in acquiring the gentle art of speaking so elegant a language.

Presently, however, he appeared more at his ease, or perhaps less inclined to talk, since he only received curt monosyllabic answers to his pleasant sallies. Five minutes had gone by without any other sound, save the spasmodic creak of Sir Percy’s pen upon the paper, the while Chauvelin and Collot watched every word he wrote.

===Chinese===

这位前大使显然正处于神经极度紧绷的状态：双手紧紧背在身后，举手投足出奇地散乱而急促。珀西·布莱克尼爵士却全然是一幅安闲自若的画中人模样：喉头的蕾丝领结打得一丝不苟，单片眼镜擎在恰到好处的角度，那件浅色的披肩大衣微微敞开，恰好露出里头精致的呢料衣装和绣工极美的背心一角。

他俨然是伦敦纨绔名流最完美的标本，倒像是正陪伴着某位贵客步入王家的客厅。当他走进烛光投下的光圈正中时，玛格丽特的目光牢牢钉在他身上；然而，纵然怀着热恋中女人那敏锐的第六感，她也察觉不出那双举着金边单片眼镜的贵胄之手有半丝颤动，也看不出那两片棱角坚毅的嘴唇有毫许翕动。

那是老贝夫鲁瓦钟楼（Beffroi）的钟敲响六点三刻时的事。此刻已将近七点，屋子正中，烛火把他的脸和身形照得通亮，珀西爵士执笔坐在桌前，正写着字。

他身后左右手边，一侧立着肖夫兰，一侧立着科洛·德埃尔布瓦，两双眼睛灼灼定定，盯着一笔一画写就的那封信。

珀西爵士似乎并不着急。他写得又慢又稳，仔细照着面前支起的那封信的底稿誊抄。有几个法文词的拼法起初像是难住了他：动笔之初，他连着说了好些自嘲打趣的话，说自己如何没受过教育、年轻时又如何疏懒，不曾学会讲这等优雅语言的高妙技艺。

不过后来他似乎自在些了，或许是懒得再说笑了——他对那些妙语只得到简短的单字回话。五分钟过去，屋里再没有别的声息，只有珀西爵士的笔尖在纸上断断续续的沙沙声；肖夫兰和科洛则盯着他写下的每一个字。

===Original===

But gradually from afar there had arisen in the stillness of evening a distant, rolling noise like that of surf breaking against the cliffs. Nearer and louder it grew, and as it increased in volume, so it gained now in diversity. The monotonous, roll-like, far-off thunder was just as continuous as before, but now shriller notes broke out from amongst the more remote sounds, a loud laugh seemed ever and anon to pierce the distance and to rise above the persistent hubbub, which became the mere accompaniment to these isolated tones.

The merrymakers of Boulogne, having started from the Place de la Sénéchaussée, were making the round of the town by the wide avenue which tops the ramparts. They were coming past the Fort Gayole, shouting, singing, brass trumpets in front, big drum ahead, drenched, hot, and hoarse, but supremely happy.

Sir Percy looked up for a moment as the noise drew nearer, then turned to Chauvelin and pointing to the letter, he said:

“I have nearly finished!”

The suspense in the smoke-laden atmosphere of this room was becoming unendurable, and four hearts at least were beating wildly with overpowering anxiety. Marguerite’s eyes were fixed with tender intensity on the man she so passionately loved. She did not understand his actions or his motives, but she felt a wild longing in her, to drink in every line of that loved face, as if with this last, long look she was bidding an eternal farewell to all hopes of future earthly happiness.

The old priest had ceased to tell his beads. Feeling in his kindly heart the echo of the appalling tragedy which was being enacted before him, he had put out a fatherly, tentative hand towards Marguerite, and given her icy fingers a comforting pressure.

And in the hearts of Chauvelin and his colleague there was satisfied revenge, eager, exultant triumph and that terrible nerve-tension which immediately precedes the long-expected climax.

But who can say what went on within the heart of that bold adventurer, about to be brought to the lowest depths of humiliation which it is in the power of man to endure? What behind that smooth unruffled brow still bent laboriously over the page of writing?

===Chinese===

可是渐渐地，从远处、从薄暮的静谧里，升起一阵滚动的闷响，好似拍岸的惊涛撞击着峭壁。它越来越近，越来越响；声势愈壮，声调也愈见繁复。那单调的、滚滚而来的远处闷雷依旧连绵不断，但更尖利的声响已从遥远的诸音之中迸发出来，一阵洪亮的哄笑不时穿透距离、盖过那片持续的喧腾，后者反倒成了这些零星声调的伴奏。

布洛涅的寻欢人群从塞内绍斯广场（Place de la Sénéchaussée）出发，正沿着墙头那条宽阔的大道环城而行。他们就要打加约勒堡垒前经过了，喊着，唱着，铜号开道，大鼓领头，个个淋得透湿、热汗腾腾、嗓子喊哑，却快活到了极点。

喧声渐近，珀西爵士抬头望了一瞬，随即转向肖夫兰，指着那封信说：

「我快写完了！」

这满屋烟气弥漫的空气里，悬望渐渐逼得人再难忍受，至少有四颗心正焦灼得狂跳不止。玛格丽特的眼睛含着深情，一瞬不瞬地钉在她炽烈爱着的那个男人身上。她不明白他的举动，也猜不透他的心思，只觉得心里涌起一阵狂热的渴望，要把那张挚爱面孔的每一道线条都深深摄进眼底——仿佛要借这最后的、悠长的一望，向今后尘世幸福的一切指望，作一次永远的诀别。

老神父已停了捻珠。他在仁厚的心里觉出眼前正上演的那出骇人悲剧的回响，便试探着向玛格丽特伸出一只慈父般的手，把她冰冷的手指安慰地握了一握。

而在肖夫兰和他同僚的心里，则是复仇得逞的餍足、急切狂喜的得意，以及久盼的高潮降临之前那可怕的神经过紧。

可是，谁能说得出，那位行将跌进人力所能忍受的屈辱最底处的大胆冒险家，心里正翻腾着什么？那依然埋头苦写的光洁平静的额头底下，又在转着什么念头？

===Original===

The crowd was now on the Place Daumont; some of the foremost in the ranks were ascending the stone steps which lead to the southern ramparts. The noise had become incessant: Pierrots and Pierrettes, Harlequins and Columbines had worked themselves up into a veritable intoxication of shouts and laughter.

Now as they all swarmed up the steps and caught sight of the open window almost on a level with the ground, and of the large dimly-lighted room, they gave forth one terrific and voluminous “Hurrah!” for the paternal government up in Paris, who had given them cause for all this joy. Then they recollected how the amnesty, the pardon, the national fête, this brilliant procession had come about, and somebody in the crowd shouted:

“*Allons!* les us have a look at that English spy!﻿ ﻿…”

“Let us see the Scarlet Pimpernel!”

“Yes! yes! let us see what he is like!”

They shouted and stamped and swarmed round the open window, swinging their lanterns and demanding in a loud tone of voice that the English spy be shown to them.

Faces wet with rain and perspiration tried to peep in at the window. Collot gave brief orders to the soldiers to close the shutters at once and to push away the crowd, but the crowd would not be pushed. It would not be gainsaid, and when the soldiers tried to close the window, twenty angry fists broke the panes of glass.

“I can’t finish this writing in your lingo, sir, whilst this demmed row is going on,” said Sir Percy placidly.

“You have not much more to write, Sir Percy,” urged Chauvelin with nervous impatience, “I pray you, finish the matter now, and get you gone from out this city.”

“Send that demmed lot away, then,” rejoined Sir Percy calmly.

“They won’t go.﻿ ﻿… They want to see you﻿ ﻿…”

Sir Percy paused a moment, pen in hand, as if in deep reflection.

“They want to see me,” he said with a laugh. “Why, demn it all﻿ ﻿… then, why not let em?﻿ ﻿…”

And with a few rapid strokes of the pen, he quickly finished the letter, adding his signature with a bold flourish, whilst the crowd, pushing, jostling, shouting and cursing the soldiers, still loudly demanded to see the Scarlet Pimpernel.

Chauvelin felt as if his heart would veritably burst with the wildness of its beating.

Then Sir Percy, with one hand lightly pressed on the letter, pushed his chair away and with his pleasant ringing voice, said once again:

===Chinese===

人群此刻已到了多蒙广场；队列里走在最前头的一些人正登上通往南面城墙的石阶。喧闹已无片刻止歇：皮埃罗和皮埃蕾特、阿尔莱坎和科隆比娜，都已喧笑得如痴如醉。

待众人蜂拥着上了石阶，一眼望见那扇几乎与地面齐平的敞开的窗、望见那间灯烛昏黄的大屋，便朝着巴黎城里慈父一般的政府，爆出一声震天动地、铺天盖地的「乌拉！」——正是那政府给了他们这一切欢腾的由头。接着他们想起，这场大赦、这道赦令、这个全国庆典、这支花团锦簇的游行队伍，究竟是从何而来的，于是人群里有人喊道：

「来呀！（Allons!）去瞧瞧那个英国间谍！……」

「让咱们看看猩红石榴红！」

「对！对！让咱们瞧瞧他生得什么模样！」

他们嚷着，跺着脚，团团围住那扇敞开的窗，摇晃着手里的灯笼，扯开嗓门叫他们把那英国间谍亮出来给他们瞧。

一张张淋着雨、浸着汗的脸挤着往窗里张望。科洛简短地下令，叫士兵立刻关上窗板、把人群推开，可人群推不开。它是不容分说的；士兵们想把窗关上，二十只怒拳便把窗玻璃砸了个粉碎。

「先生，这么该死的吵个没完，您这门法国话我实在没法子再写下去啦。」珀西爵士平心静气地说。

「您没剩多少要写的了，珀西爵士，」肖夫兰焦躁难耐地催促道，「求您这就写完，也好离开这座城。」

「那就把那帮该死的家伙打发走。」珀西爵士不动声色地回敬道。

「他们不走。……他们要见您……」

珀西爵士执笔在手，顿了片刻，仿佛在深思。

「他们要见我，」他朗声笑道，「咦，这也该死的……那么，干吗不让他们见？……」

说罢笔走龙蛇，几下便写完了那封信，又遒劲潇洒地挥笔签上名字；这时人群推推搡搡，一面冲着士兵叫骂，一面仍在高声嚷着要看一看猩红石榴红。

肖夫兰只觉得自己的心简直要狂跳得炸裂开来。

接着，珀西爵士一手轻轻按住那封信，把椅子往后一推，用他那悦耳洪亮的嗓音，又说道：

===Original===

“Well! demn it﻿ ﻿… let ’em see me!﻿ ﻿…”

With that he sprang to his feet and up to his full height, and as he did so he seized the two massive pewter candlesticks, one in each hand, and with powerful arms well outstretched he held them high above his head.

“The letter﻿ ﻿…” murmured Chauvelin in a hoarse whisper.

But even as he was quickly reaching out a hand, which shook with the intensity of his excitement, towards the letter on the table, Blakeney, with one loud and sudden shout, threw the heavy candlesticks onto the floor. They rattled down with a terrific crash, the lights were extinguished, and the whole room was immediately plunged in utter darkness.

The crowd gave a wild yell of fear: they had only caught sight for one instant of that gigantic figure﻿—which, with arms outstretched had seemed supernaturally tall﻿—weirdly illumined by the flickering light of the tallow candles and the next moment disappearing into utter darkness before their very gaze. Overcome with sudden superstitious fear, Pierrots and Pierrettes, drummer and trumpeters, turned and fled in every direction.

Within the room all was wild confusion. The soldiers had heard a cry:

“*La fenêtre! La fenêtre!*”

Who gave it no one knew, no one could afterwards recollect: certain it is that with one accord the majority of the men made a rush for the open window, driven thither partly by the wild instinct of the chase after an escaping enemy, and partly by the same superstitious terror which had caused the crowd to flee. They clambered over the sill and dropped down on to the ramparts below, then started in wild pursuit.

But when the crash came, Chauvelin had given one frantic shout:

“The letter!!!﻿ ﻿… *Collot!!*﻿ ﻿… *A moi.*﻿ ﻿… In his hand.﻿ ﻿… The letter!﻿ ﻿…”

There was the sound of a heavy thud, of a terrible scuffle there on the floor in the darkness and then a yell of victory from Collot d’Herbois.

“I have the letter! A Paris!”

“Victory!” echoed Chauvelin, exultant and panting, “victory!! The Angelus, friend Hébert! Take the *calotin* to ring the Angelus!!!”

===Chinese===

「好哇！该死的……就让他们见我！……」

话音未落，他一跃而起，挺直了整个身子；就在这一跃之间，他两手各攫住一只沉重的白镴烛台，强健的双臂奋力张开，把它们高高举过头顶。

「那封信……」肖夫兰嗄着嗓子低低唤道。

可就在他激动得发抖的手迅速伸向桌上那封信的当口，布莱克尼猛喝一声，把那两只沉重的烛台掷到地上。烛台哐啷啷砸落，一声骇人的巨响，烛火应声而灭，整间屋子霎时陷入一团漆黑。

人群爆出一声惊怖的狂喊：他们只来得及瞥见那么一瞬——那巨硕的身影，双臂张开，高得仿佛超乎自然，在牛油烛摇曳不定的光里诡异地照亮，转眼便当着他们的目光消失在全然的黑暗里。皮埃罗和皮埃蕾特、鼓手和号手，被这突如其来的迷信恐惧攫住，掉头向四面八方逃散了。

屋内则是一片大乱。士兵们听见一声喊：

「窗口！（La fenêtre!）窗口！」

这一声是谁喊的，没人知道，事后也没人记得起来；可以肯定的只是：士兵们大都不约而同地朝那扇敞开的窗口直冲过去，一半是受了追捕逃敌的狂热本能的驱使，一半也是被那教人群逃散的迷信恐惧所推动。他们翻过窗台，跳落到下面的城墙上，随即发足狂追。

而方才巨响炸开的一刻，肖夫兰也曾狂喊过一声：

「信！！！……*科洛*！！……*来人*！（A moi）……在他手里。……那封信！……」

黑暗里，地板上响起一记沉重的闷撞、一场凶狠的扭打，接着便是科洛·德埃尔布瓦一声得胜的高呼。

「信到手了！去巴黎！」

「胜利了！」肖夫兰喘着气，狂喜地应声喊道，「胜利了！！敲晚祷钟，埃贝尔朋友！带这*calotin*（教士佬）去敲晚祷钟！！！」

===Original===

It was instinct which caused Collot d’Herbois to find the door; he tore it open, letting in a feeble ray of light from the corridor. He stood in the doorway one moment, his slouchy, ungainly form distinctly outlined against the lighter background beyond, a look of exultant and malicious triumph, of deadly hate and cruelty distinctly imprinted on his face and with upraised hand wildly flourishing the precious document, the brand of dishonour for the enemy of France.

“A Paris!” shouted Chauvelin to him excitedly. “Into Robespierre’s hands.﻿ ﻿… The letter!﻿ ﻿…”

Then he fell back panting, exhausted on the nearest chair.

Collot, without looking again behind him, called wildly for the men who were to escort him to Paris. They were picked troopers, stalwart veterans from the old municipal guard. They had not broken their ranks throughout the turmoil, and fell into line in perfect order as they followed Citizen Collot out of the room.

Less than five minutes later there was the noise of stamping and champing of bits in the courtyard below, a shout from Collot, and the sound of a cavalcade galloping at breakneck speed towards the distant Paris gate.

===Chinese===

是本能引着科洛·德埃尔布瓦摸到了门；他一把扯开门，放进走廊里一线微光。他在门口站了一瞬，那松垮笨拙的身形，在身后较亮的背景上轮廓分明；脸上分明印着狂喜而恶毒的得胜之色、致命的仇恨与残忍；高举的手里，狂乱地挥舞着那份珍贵的文书——法兰西之敌的耻辱烙印。

「去巴黎！」肖夫兰冲他兴奋地大喊，「交到罗伯斯庇尔手里。……那封信！……」

喊罢，他喘息着，颓然倒在最近的一把椅子上。

科洛没有再回头看一眼，扬声唤来那队要护送他去巴黎的兵。他们是精选出来的骑兵，旧市卫队里魁伟的老兵。方才那场大乱里他们队列不乱，此刻随着科洛公民鱼贯出屋，行列整肃。

不出五分钟，楼下院子里便响起马蹄刨地、嚼环衔铁的声响，科洛一声吆喝，一队人马朝着远处巴黎城门的方向，没命地飞驰而去。

## XXXIV. The Angelus / XXXIV. 晚祷钟

===Original===
And gradually all noises died away around the old Fort Gayole. The shouts and laugher of the merrymakers, who had quickly recovered from their fright, now came only as the muffled rumble of a distant storm, broken here and there by the shrill note of a girl’s loud laughter, or a vigorous fanfare from the brass trumpets.

===Chinese===
渐渐地，古老的加约勒堡垒四周，一切喧声都沉寂了下去。那班寻欢作乐的人早已从惊吓中缓过神来，他们的呼喝与哄笑，此刻听来只剩远方风暴一般沉闷的低吼，间或有一串姑娘家响亮的笑声尖厉地划破，或是铜号齐鸣，吹出一段雄壮的调子。

===Original===
The room where so much turmoil had taken place, where so many hearts had beaten with torrent-like emotions, where the awesome tragedy of revenge and hate, of love and passion had been consummated, was now silent and at peace.

===Chinese===
这间屋子，曾掀起过那许多骚乱，曾有那许多颗心怀着激流般的情绪怦怦狂跳，曾演成过复仇与憎恨、爱情与热望那惊心动魄的悲剧——如今却归于寂静与安宁。

===Original===
The soldiers had gone: some in pursuit of the revellers, some with Collot d’Herbois, others with Hébert and the *calotin* who was to ring the Angelus.

===Chinese===
士兵们都走了：有的去追赶寻欢的人群，有的随科洛·德埃尔布瓦去了，还有些跟着埃贝尔（Hébert），押着那个要去敲晚祷钟的教士佬（*calotin*）。

===Original===
Chauvelin, overcome with the intensity of his exultation and the agony of the suspense which he had endured, sat, vaguely dreaming, hardly conscious, but wholly happy and content. Fearless, too, for his triumph was complete, and he cared not now if he lived or died.

===Chinese===
